 VersaWorks Settings

## Page 8

Setting the Number of Simultaneous RIP Processing Operations
When multiple printers are registered in VersaWorks, you can set the number of RIP processing operations
to perform simultaneously.
[Max. # of concurrent RIP processes] varies depending on the number of registered printers. If two
printers are registered, you can select a value up to 2. If three printers are registered, you can select a
value up to 3. If four to eight printers are registered, you can select a value up to 4.
For example, if there are three jobs that you want to perform RIP processing on, specifying 2 allows for
simultaneous RIP processing on two of the three jobs. Then, when one RIP processing operation finishes,
the RIP processing of the remaining jobs starts. However, when a large number is set, the amount of
computer CPU usage and memory usage increase.
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [RIP and Print].
3.
Select the number of simultaneous RIP processes from [Max. # of concurrent RIP processes] under [RIP
Speed Settings].
When there is one registered printer, the default value is 1. When there are two to eight registered
printers, the default value is 2.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
Efficiency Settings
VersaWorks Settings
7

## Page 9

Setting the Number of Simultaneous Printing Operations
When multiple printers are connected to VersaWorks, you can set the number of printing operations to
perform simultaneously.
For example, configure this setting when you want to print simultaneously using multiple printers. When
you are printing on two printers, specifying a value of 1 results in data being transmitted sequentially
to one printer and then to the other. If a value of 2 is specified, data is transmitted to two printers
simultaneously, and simultaneous printing begins. When a large number is set, the amount of computer
CPU usage and memory usage increase.
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [RIP and Print].
3.
Select the number of simultaneous printing operations from [Max. # of Concurrent Outputs] under [Print
Options].
The default value is 1.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
Efficiency Settings
8
 VersaWorks Settings

## Page 10

Printing Only Once at All Times
Print the specified job only once at all times without specifying the print count.
When this setting is used (turned off), printing will begin immediately when [Print] is clicked, eliminating
the hassle of specifying the print count each time.
With the default value of on, a window for specifying the print count is displayed each time when [Print] is
clicked. The print count can be specified for job groups and layered jobs.
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [RIP and Print].
3.
Clear the [Configure Advanced Print Settings] checkbox under [Print Options].
This checkbox is selected by default.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
Efficiency Settings
VersaWorks Settings
9

## Page 11

Setting Warning Displays According to the Amount of Remaining Ink
With the default settings, a warning is displayed at print start when the amount of ink remaining is low.
However, depending on the print data, it may be possible to complete printing. You can configure the
settings so that, by determining the amount of ink to use, this warning is not displayed during operation.
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [RIP and Print].
3.
Clear the [Show warning about ink shortage on print command] checkbox under [Print Options].
This checkbox is selected by default.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
Useful Settings

 VersaWorks Settings

## Page 12

Customizing the Display of the [Settings] and [Queue Properties] Tabs
You can show/hide or reorder the [Settings] and [Queue Properties] tabs ([Layout], [Quality], etc.) for
each registered printer. The display can be customized, such as by reordering the tabs in order of the
frequency of use, or hiding tabs that are not changed or used.
When set with [Settings] or with [Queue Properties], it is applied to all queue folders and jobs for that
printer.
Procedure
1.
Select the printer to set, and then open the [Settings] or [Queue Properties] tab.
• [Settings]
• Double-click the job.
• Right-click a job, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• Click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
 [Settings].
• [Queue Properties]: Click
 for the queue folder.
2.
Click
 [Customize].
The [Tab Display Settings] window appears.
3.
Follow the procedure below to customize the display of the printing setting tabs.
• Hiding printing setting tabs
a. Select the target tab from the [Tabs to Show and Their Order] list, and then click
.
The target tab is moved to [Tabs to Hide].
b. To return to [Tabs to Show and Their Order], select the target tab and click
.
The target tab is moved to the bottom of [Tabs to Show and Their Order].
• Changing the order of the printing setting tabs
Select the target tab from the [Tabs to Show and Their Order] list, and then click [Move Up]
or [Move Down] to change the order.
• Hiding tab names
Unselect the [Show Labels] checkbox.
Click [Reset] to restore tab display/hide and reordering to the defaults.
MEMO
4.
Click [OK].
You are returned to the [Settings] or [Queue Properties] window.
5.
Click [OK].
You are returned to the main window.

## Page 13

Setting the Measurement Instrument to Use
When using a measurement instrument, set the type of measurement instrument to use for [For Color
Charts], [For Spot and Special Colors], and [For Printer Color Matching] measurement.
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [Measurement Instrument].
3.
According to the application, select the measurement instrument to use from [Model] of the following
items.
• [For Color Charts]
• [For Spot and Special Colors]
• [For Printer Color Matching]*1
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
*1
Select from [Use [For Color Charts]] or [VW-S1]. VW-S1 can be used with Windows only.
Other Settings
12
 VersaWorks Settings

## Page 14

Increasing Memory
With a large quantity of image data, a memory shortage may occur, which may result in processing by the
RIP and output becoming slow or impossible, or prevent print data from being added to the job list. In this
case, increase the amount of memory.
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [RIP and Print].
3.
Adjust [Memory Amount].
Drag the slider or click its left and right sides to change the amount of memory.
The default value is 1024 MB.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
Other Settings
VersaWorks Settings
13

## Page 15

Hiding the Queue Folder Identification in the Layout Window
Click
, displayed in the top-right corner of the job layout area of the main window, to display
previews of all the jobs in the queue folder on the selected printer.
With the initial settings, the queue folder name (A, B, C, ...) is displayed in the preview image so that you
can tell which queue folder is being displayed in these preview images. It can be hidden in cases such as
when a part of the preview image is covered, or when multiple queue folders are not used.
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [General].
3.
Clear the [Show Queue Identifier in Layout Window] checkbox under [Queue].
This checkbox is selected by default.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.

14
 VersaWorks Settings

## Page 16

Preventing Update Notifications
VersaWorks updates several times a year. Updates will be made to support new models, to add new
features, media or color profiles, and to fix bugs. With the initial settings, the function for notifying
the user of available updates when VersaWorks starts is enabled. However, if updates other than adding
printers will not be performed, you can disable update notifications.
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [Update].
3.
Clear the [Check for Updates Automatically and Notify] checkbox under [Update Settings].
This checkbox is selected by default.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
Other Settings
VersaWorks Settings
15

## Page 17

Checking the Link Settings with External Applications
When the following application software links with VersaWorks, this setting must be on.
Turning this setting off will prevent the "Stop Connect Hub" message that is displayed when Connect Hub is
not activated. As a result, functions added through Connect are disabled. Also, the message informing the
user that additional features have been disabled will no longer be displayed.
• Connect Hub
• FlexiDESIGNER
Procedure
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [Linking].
3.
Check that [Link with External Application] under [Linking] is on.
This checkbox is selected by default.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
Other Settings

 VersaWorks Settings

## Page 18

VersaWorks updates several times a year. Updates will be made to support new models, to add new
features, media or color profiles, and to fix bugs. We recommend that you always update to the latest
version of VersaWorks so that you can use the highly-functional VersaWorks.
VersaWorks is updated over an Internet connection. However, it may take some time to download and
install the update file depending on your operating environment.
Select the update method below according to your VersaWorks use status. You can only update to the
latest version available at the time of the update.
• Update to the latest version at all times.
P. 17 Using the Latest Version of VersaWorks
• Check the contents of the update via automatic notification and decide whether to update or not
update.
P. 19 Checking the Details of Updates and Updating When an Automatic Notification Occurs
• Check the contents of the update at any time you want and decide whether to update or not update.
P. 21 Checking the Details of Updates and Updating at the Desired Time
Using the Latest Version of VersaWorks
Use this section to configure the settings so a notification is provided when VersaWorks starts if a new
version is available. When this notification (the [Online Update] window) is displayed, you can update to
use the latest version of VersaWorks.
Procedure
Check settings
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Preferences].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [Update].
3.
Select the [Check for Updates Automatically and Notify] checkbox under [Update Settings].
This checkbox is selected by default.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
5.
Exit VersaWorks.
Update
6.
Start VersaWorks.
• If a new version is not available, no notification will appear. You are using the latest version.
• If a new version is available, the [Online Update] window will appear with the details of the
update. Carry out the following steps to update the software.
Update
VersaWorks Settings
17

## Page 19

In the [Online Update] window, you can also select one of the following options instead of [Install Update],
which will immediately update the software to the latest version.
• [Skip This Version]
Skips this update to the latest version. Notifications will not be displayed until the next time the latest
version is released.
• [Notify Me Later]
Closes the notification window and prevents notifications from being displayed for 24 hours. Thereafter,
the notification will be displayed when VersaWorks is started.
MEMO
7.
Click [Install Update].
The [Online Update] window appears, and the required data is downloaded.
8.
When the message [Ready to install.] appears, click [Install Update.]
VersaWorks is exited, and the introductory window of the installer appears. Follow the on-screen
instructions to carry out the installation.
Update

 VersaWorks Settings

## Page 20

Performing Updates According to Their Details
You can perform updates according to their notified details. You can also manually check the details of and
perform updates.
Checking the Details of Updates and Updating When an Automatic Notification
Occurs
Updates are performed according to the contents of notifications. First, use this section to configure
the settings so a notification is provided when VersaWorks starts if a new version is available. When this
notification window appears, check the Update Details and determine whether to update.
Procedure
Check settings
1.
Display [Preferences] or [Settings].
• Windows: From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Preferences].
• Mac: Click [VersaWorks 7]>[Preferences] on the menu bar.
2.
Click [Update].
3.
Select the [Check for Updates Automatically and Notify] checkbox under [Update Settings].
This checkbox is selected by default.
4.
Click [OK].
The [Preferences] or [Preferences] window closes.
5.
Exit VersaWorks.
Check update
6.
Start VersaWorks.
• If a new version is not available, no notification will appear. You are using the latest version.
• If a new version is available, the [Online Update] window will appear with the details of the
update. To update the software, carry out the following steps.
In the [Online Update] window, you can also select one of the following options instead of [Install Update],
which will immediately update the software to the latest version.
• [Skip This Version]
Skips this update to the latest version. Notifications will not be displayed until the next time the latest
version is released.
• [Notify Me Later]
Closes the notification window and prevents notifications from being displayed for 24 hours. Thereafter,
the notification will be displayed when VersaWorks is started.

Click [Install Update].
The [Online Update] window appears, and the required data is downloaded.

When the message [Ready to install.] appears, click [Install Update.]

## Page 24

Saving VersaWorks Settings and Jobs for Later Use
Various VersaWorks settings can be reproduced on new computers from data backed up from the original
computer when changing computers. The following data can be backed up.
• Preferences or settings
• Printer Status
• Jobs (Job information used in VersaWorks)
• Important files (User-defined spot colors, special colors, and media; printing settings files; color-
matching data; Nearest Color Finder; etc.)
• Print data (PDF files, EPS files) should be backed up separately.
• Backup data cannot be imported if the VersaWorks version is older than the version used to create the backup data.
MEMO
Saving Backup Data
Save the VersaWorks settings and job backup data.
Procedure
1.
In an arbitrary location, create a folder where the backup data should be stored.
Make sure the folder is empty.
2.
Exit VersaWorks.
3.
Start VersaWorks [VW7 Data Management].
• Windows 11 (version: 24H2)
Click [Start]>[All] or [All apps], and then click [VersaWorks 7]>[VW7 Data Management].
• Mac (macOS 15.2)
Click [Finder] under [Dock], then click [Applications]>[VW7 Data Management].
The [VersaInit] window appears.
4.
Click [Back Up].
5.
Click [...], and then specify the folder created in Step 1.
When you specify the folder, you are returned to the [VersaInit] window.
6.
Click [Back Up].
7.
In the backup confirmation window, click [Yes].
The backup data will be saved in the specified folder.
8.
When a completion message appears, click [OK].
9.
Click [Close].
The [VersaInit] window closes.
Backing Up Data

## Page 26

Initializing VersaWorks
VersaWorks may fail to start correctly if data corruption occurs. It may also freeze while configuring jobs
settings, etc. In such cases, recovery may be possible from [VW7 Data Management].
First, delete the jobs and printing settings. If VersaWorks is not restored, initialize all settings, excluding
important data. If VersaWorks still cannot be restored, delete the operation data (important data) from
before startup failed.
Step 1: Initializing the Job and Print Properties
Procedure
1.
Exit VersaWorks.
2.
Start VersaWorks [VW7 Data Management].
• Windows 11 (version: 24H2)
Click [Start]>[All] or [All apps], and then click [VersaWorks 7]>[VW7 Data Management].
• Mac (macOS 15.2)
Click [Finder] under [Dock], then click [Applications]>[VW7 Data Management].
The [VersaInit] window appears.
3.
Click [Initialize].
The initialization window appears.
4.
Select [Job and Print Properties], and then click [Initialize].
5.
In the confirmation window, click [Yes].
The job and print properties are initialized.
6.
Click [OK].
7.
Click [Close].
The [VersaInit] window closes.
8.
Start VersaWorks.
The operation is completed if VersaWorks is started successfully.
If VersaWorks still cannot be started, proceed to the next step.
Initialization

Exit VersaWorks.

• Mac (macOS 15.2)
Click [Finder] under [Dock], then click [Applications]>[VW7 Data Management].

When two or more computers are connected to the network, you can output from another computer (A) on
which the print data was created or edited via a computer (B) on which VersaWorks is installed.
In this situation, the computer on which VersaWorks is installed is called the "RIP server," and other
computers are called "clients."
Transmit the print data created on a client to the RIP server (1). Although an image file transmitted from
a client is sent to the printer via the RIP server (2), the RIP server works as a printer when viewed from a
client.

Outputting from the Client
Print from the client via the RIP server (the computer on which VersaWorks is installed: Windows only).
First, check the following items:
• The printer and the computers (RIP server and client) are connected correctly and are all switched on.
• The printer is set on the RIP server (the printer is registered in VersaWorks).
• The printer is ready to print (media has been set up).
• The client is a Mac OS X (10.5 or later) machine.
1. Preparing the RIP Server
Use the [Queue Properties] window to set the way to process the print data received from clients.

1.
Click
 for Queue A.
The [Queue Properties] window appears.

Click
[Job Management] and then use [Action] under [Action for Incoming Jobs] to set the way to
process the jobs added to the RIP server.

## Page 30

• [Do Nothing]: Neither processing by the RIP nor printing is performed when a job is added to
the job list.
• [RIP Only]: Only processing by the RIP is automatically performed when a job is added to the
job list.
• [RIP and Print]: Both processing by the RIP and printing are automatically performed when a
job is added to the job list.
3.
Click [OK].
You are returned to the main window.

Adobe Illustrator

Click [File]>[Print] from the menu bar.

Set the number of copies and paper size as necessary.

Configure the necessary settings on the VersaWorks (RIP server) side and print.

Outputting Using a Hot Folder
This section explains how to set the transfer of print data saved in a specified folder to the VersaWorks
queue folder. Configuring this setting when print data is created on a computer that does not have
VersaWorks installed, or when there are multiple print data creators, allows print data to be easily loaded
into VersaWorks.
VersaWorks
With this setting configured, you can create nested jobs in the queue folder and you can configure RIP and
printing settings to allow for automation.
Print data saved in a hot folder will be deleted from the hot folder after transfer, so the source data should be saved
separately.

## Page 38

Checking and Managing the Job Log Information
You can view the information about jobs processed in VersaWorks, such as their contents and the date and
time when they were processed, in the [Job Log] window. Jobs can be sorted, filtered, and deleted in this
window. Detailed information about the log of each job can also be checked.
You can save the log information to a CSV file or as XML file format. You can open the saved data with
spreadsheet software or other software, and use it to check ink consumption and print time.
Viewing the Job Log Information
Display the [Job Log] window.
Procedure
1.
From the menu bar, click [View]>[Job Log].
The [Job Log] window appears. This window includes the following information.
[No.]
Order in which the print data was added
[Event]
Type of process that is being performed or that has been completed
[Nick Name]
Printer model name or the nickname
[Job Name]
Print data name
[Size]
Print data size
[Print Area]
Size of the printed width and height
[Ink Consumption]
Amount of ink used to print*1
[Start Time]
Date and time that the process shown in [Event] was started
[End Time]
Date and time that the process shown in [Event] finished
[Details]
Information such as the error category and cause (if an error occurs during the processing of the
job)
 
*1
Displayed when processing by the RIP is performed.
 
• In the [Job Log] window, click a column title in the table such as [Event], [Nick Name], or [Job Name] to
sort that item in ascending or descending order.
• Saving the Job Log Information(P. 39)The above is available.

To filter [Job Log], click
.
The [Filter Conditions] window appears. You can filter on the following items.
• [Date]
• [Nick Name]
• [Job Name]
• [Setting]
• [Processing Result]
3.
Click [OK].
Jobs appear according to the filtering conditions.

Under [Position], perform one of the following operations:
• Enter the values in [X] and [Y].
The job is moved by the specified offset in relation to the job list layout area window's upper
left corner (lower right of set up media). [X] sets the right distance and [Y] sets the downward
distance. The actual printing will be output rotated 180°, so horizontal movement will be to
the left and vertical movement will be to the top.
• Select the [Center on the Media] checkbox.
When using roll media, the job is moved to the horizontal center of the media. When using
sheet media or standard-size media, the job is moved to the horizontal and vertical center of
the media.
When changes are made in [Settings], you can check the changed position in the layout area.
MEMO
4.
Click [OK].
• Any changes made in [Settings] will be applied, and you will be returned to the main window.
• When changes are made in [Queue Properties]:
• When a message for saving the printing settings appears, click [OK].
• To apply the changes to a job already in the queue folder, right-click the job and click
[Copy/Paste Setting Value]>[Apply Current Queue Settings]. The changes are reflected in
the layout area of the main window.
If, after printing, you are performing laminating followed by cutting (block & cut), clear the [Center on the
Media] checkbox. If the [Center on the Media] checkbox is selected, the cutting position may be misaligned.
MEMO

## Page 59

Adjusting Print Head Height to Match Media Thickness
This section explains how to adjust the print head height to match the media thickness.
The height of the print head is set with [Quality]>[Quality Settings]>[Media Name]. Changing this height
may affect the printing quality. Normally do not change this setting.
However when printing on thick media and when swelling occurred due to drying after printing, the
distance between the print heads and the print surface will be smaller than the intended value. Depending
on the circumstances, the print head may contact the media. In order to avoid this, change the height of
the print head. If the print head height on the printer and the setting are different, a message may be
displayed on the printer. In this situation, make the settings the same.
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.

[Settings].

 for the queue folder.
The [Queue Properties] appears.
2.
Click
 [Printer Controls].
3.
Select [Use Custom Settings] for [Other Controls].
4.
Set the [Head Height].
5.
Click [OK].
• Any changes made in [Settings] will be applied, and you will be returned to the main window.
• When changes are made in [Queue Properties]:
• When a message for saving the printing settings appears, click [OK].

## Page 62

Correcting the Dimensions in the Printing Results
Print data and printing size may change due to factors such as thermal expansion of the media after
printing. In this case, you can correct a shifted printing result by correcting the horizontal and vertical
sizes of the job dimensions.
If you enter the measured sizes (the actual print size) and the expected sizes (the size to be printed), the
correction factors can be automatically calculated and easily set. You can also enter the correction factors
directly.
For print data with cutting lines, the print dimensions can be corrected, but the cutting line dimensions
cannot be corrected.
Procedure
1.
Measure the size of the printed output.
2.
Use one of the methods below to open the [Settings] window.
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
3.
Click
 [Printer Controls].
4.
Click [Correction Calculator] under [Print Size Correction].
The [Correction Calculator] window appears.
5.
In [Expected Size] and [Measured Size], enter the sizes in the horizontal and vertical directions.
• [Expected Size]: Enter the size to be printed.
• [Measured Size]: Enter the measured size of the printing result.
The input value is calculated automatically and displayed in [Correction Factor]. However, the
correction factors can be set within a range of −5.00% to +5.00%.
6.
Click [OK].
The [Correction Calculator] window closes. Verify that the correction factor is reflected in the
horizontal and vertical values.
7.
Click [OK].
You are returned to the main window.
• You can also enter the correction factors directly.
• You can also configure the same setting in the [Queue Properties] window. After setting, to apply the
changes to a job already in the queue folder, right-click on the job and click [Copy/Paste Setting Val-
ue]>[Apply Current Queue Settings].

## Page 65

Limiting Print Heads Used for Printing: [Nozzle Mask]
When discharge issues on the printer such as dot drop-out and dot displacement are not improved,
partially restrict the print heads (nozzles) from being used for printing (i.e. prevent the use of the print
heads that are causing discharge issues). This operation is called the "nozzle mask," and while it slows
down printing speed, it also allows you to decrease the impact of dot drop-out or dot displacement. This is
an emergency measure that should be used when you have to prioritize order delivery.
First, use the printer's menu to perform a printing test for the nozzle mask. With this printing test, a test
pattern is printed for each nozzle group. The number of groups varies depending on the printer.
The top row of the following table shows the nozzles to mask, and the bottom row shows test patterns.
From the left, the case where the lower half of the nozzles is masked (1), the case where the upper half
of the nozzles is masked (2), and the case where the upper and lower 1/4 of the nozzles are masked (3)
are shown.
Example of nozzle masking
Nozzles to mask*1
Nozzle mask test pattern
 
*1
The gray parts in the figure show the print head parts that are not used (masked).
 
According to the printing test result, print with the nozzle group—(1), (2), or (3)—that has the least
discharge issues.
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.
Procedure
1.
Perform a printing test for the nozzle mask.
For details on how to perform printing tests, refer to the instruction manual for your printer.
2.
Click
 [Home].
3.
Click
 to the right of the target printer, and then click [Change Nozzle Mask].
4.
Select the nozzle group to use in printing from the printing-test results.
5.
Click [OK].
The [Change Nozzle Mask] window closes.
 is added to the status icon of the set printer.
When printing for which delivery is prioritized is finished, take action to correct the discharge defects.
Printer Control Settings
64
 Job and Queue Folder Settings

## Page 66

Setting the Halftone
The following two RIP processing methods are available.
• [Dither]: A screening method that uses a specific dithering pattern to yield fast processing by the RIP.
It results in comparatively stable colors.
• [Error Diffusion]: The finishing of finer details such as the granulation and the thin line image quality
improves, but the time required for processing by the RIP is longer than when [Dither] is used. Rarely,
this method may result in strange colors.
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.
Procedure
1.
Perform the operation shown below according to what is being set.
• Job
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click

## Page 67

Selecting the Interpolation Used for Scaling
The following three interpolation methods are available when scaling the image.
• [Nearest Neighbor]: The image easily becomes coarse when it is zoomed in on and blurry when it is
zoomed out from, but the processing speed is high. The accuracy is lowest.
• [Bi-Linear]: Standard quality and processing speed.
• [Bi-Cubic]: Lowest image degradation during scaling but low processing speed.

 next to [Details].

## Page 68

Setting the Direction of the Print Head
You can configure one of the following two settings with [Direction] on models whose print head can print
in the outbound and return passes.
• [Bi-Direction]: When the print head moves, printing will be performed in both the outbound pass and
return pass. The printing time is shorter than [Uni-Direction], but printing is affected by the thickness
of the media, so you have to adjust the misalignment of the ink landing position on the machine.
• [Uni-Direction]: Printing will only be performed in the outbound pass (depending on the model,
printing may only be performed in the return pass). The printing time is longer but the quality is
higher. With this setting, there is no need to make adjustments on the machine.
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.

• Job

[Settings].

 for the queue folder.
The [Queue Properties] appears.

Click
 [Quality].
3.
Select the media to use in printing from [Media Name] under [Quality Settings].
4.
Use [Print Quality] to select the printing quality.
If [Resolution] and [Mode] are selectable, select those as well.
The available parameters for [Resolution] and [Mode] vary depending on the media setting in the
[Media Explorer] window.
5.

 next to [Details].

## Page 70

Setting the Optimizer Ink Amount
The printing quality of water-based resin inks is affected by factors such as the media, temperature, and
humidity.
It is recommended to perform test printing with multiple optimizer ink levels and set the optimizer ink
amount to the level that provides the optimal image quality before performing actual printing.
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.
If jobs with different optimizer ink levels have been set as a nested job, the message [A different value has been set
for each job.] appears.
To perform actual printing, not test printing, with a nested job, set all the jobs to the same optimizer ink level.

## Page 71

Reducing the Amount of Media Used
Optimize media usage by arranging multiple jobs to minimize the total media usage.
Nest
The process of arranging multiple jobs to minimize the total media usage is called nesting. Also, a group of
jobs nested together is called a nested job.
By effectively nesting jobs, you can minimize the amount of media that is used.
Creating a Nested Job from the Selected Jobs
Create nested jobs by selecting multiple jobs from the job list.
Among all the selected jobs, the settings of the first job that was added to the job list are applied to the print settings
of the nested job.
MEMO
Procedure
1.
Select multiple jobs from the job list.
The method for selecting multiple jobs depends on the OS.
2.
Use one of the methods below to create a nested job.
• Right-click the selected multiple jobs, and then click [Compile Jobs]>[Nest].
• With multiple jobs selected, click
 on the right side of the job tool area, and then click
[Nest].
A nested job is created from the selected jobs. The name of the nested job will be [Nest Job
Date-Number]. Serial numbers (-1, -2...) are assigned according to the creation order.
• First nested job: [Nest Job 12/8-1]
• Second nested job: [Nest Job 12/8-2]
Efficiency Settings

## Page 72

Automatically Creating a Nested Job
It is possible to set the conditions and automatically create nested jobs.
The examples used in this explanation are cases in which there is more than one creator of print data.
Print data is regularly loaded into VersaWorks via the network. Set processing conditions for loaded print
data and create nested jobs. You can also automate processing by the RIP or even printing, if necessary.
The print data processing conditions are as follows.
• [By Time]
When the specified time is reached, a nested job is created.
• [By Paper Length]
A nested job is created to the specified length.
• [By Number of Jobs]
A nested job is created when the specified number is reached.
If [Action] under [Action for Incoming Jobs] has been used to set [RIP Only] or [RIP and Print], processing
by the RIP or printing will be performed when the nested jobs are completed.
Procedure
1.
Click
 for the queue folder to configure settings.
The [Queue Properties] window appears.
2.
Configure the printing settings such as the media and print quality.
3.
Click
 [Job Management].
4.
With [Jobs are automatically nested on the following conditions.], turn on the conditions to specify, then
enter the values.
[By Time]
When the specified time is reached, a nested job is created. If you set [Set Time Interval],
nested jobs are created according to the set interval of time.
[By Paper Length]
A nested job is created to the specified length. You also will not be able to check the state of
nesting in the layout window until the nested job is finished.
For example, if you set [By Paper Length] to 1000.00 mm, a nested job for 1 m is created. At
this point, the state of nesting is displayed in the layout window.
However, until the nested job is created, jobs are listed in the order that they are added to the
job list even if 1 m is reached before nesting, as shown on the left side of the diagram shown
below.

## Page 74

Automating Processing by the RIP and Printing
This section explains how to configure the settings so that jobs added to the job list are automatically
processed by the RIP, printed, or processed after printing.
Processing a Job by the RIP or Printing a Job Automatically
Sets the operation when print data is added to the job list.
[Do Nothing] is the default setting, but the amount of time required can be shortened by setting [RIP
Only] or [RIP and Print].
However, [RIP and Print] should be used with caution, as print data is printed using the [Queue Proper-
ties] settings.
Procedure
1.
Click
 for the queue folder to configure settings.
The [Queue Properties] window appears.
2.
Click
 [Job Management].
3.
From [Action] under [Action for Incoming Jobs], select one of the following.
[RIP Only]
Processing by the RIP is automatically performed when a job is added to the job list.
[RIP and Print]
Both processing by the RIP and printing are automatically performed when a job is added to the
job list. With this setting, you cannot specify the print count.
Select the [Auto Rotate to Save Media] checkbox to reduce the amount of wasted media by automatically
placing portrait images horizontally. This function operates independently from what is selected for [Action].
If you select this checkbox, jobs will be rotated and arranged on the media automatically even if [Do Nothing]
is selected for [Action].
MEMO
4.
Set other items as necessary.
5.
Click [OK].
The [Queue Properties] window closes.
6.
Add the print data to the job list.
The set process is performed automatically.

## Page 75

Setting Processing after Printing Is Completed
This section explains how to process a job after printing is completed.
• [Save Job]
Jobs will be saved after printing finishes.
• [Delete RIP Data]
All intermediate data such as the data processed by the RIP will be deleted, but the job will remain in
the job list.
• [Delete Job]
Jobs will be deleted completely and will be deleted from the job list.
Procedure
1.
Click
 for the queue folder to configure settings.
The [Queue Properties] window appears.
2.
Click
 [Job Management].
3.
Select the processing method from [Action After Printing] under [RIP and Print].
[Save Job]*1
The data processed by the RIP is kept, so you can print the job again without having to process it
by the RIP. However, as more jobs are added to the job list, finding jobs involves more effort.
[Delete RIP Data]
To print the job again, you will have to process it by the RIP again.
VersaWorks will have less data capacity than if [Save Job] is used, but the number of jobs in the
job list will increase.
[Delete Job]
When the output is finished, the job is deleted and the queue folder is emptied. To print the job
again, the print data must be saved separately.
 
*1
Default value
 
4.
Click [OK].
The [Queue Properties] window closes.
When you are changing the settings so that they are different for different jobs, you can add them to the job
list and then change them in the [Settings] window.
MEMO

## Page 77

Outputting Multiple Jobs at Once
This section explains how to group multiple jobs and thereby output them with a single operation.
• Example 1
Group together print data A, print data B, and print data C and print three copies. Because the printed
materials will be in the sequence A-B-C, A-B-C, and A-B-C, you can produce 3 copies of the print
object.
• Example 2
By grouping white print data, CMYK print data, and gloss print data and performing pullback printing
with the white and CMYK print data, you can obtain layered printing in the order of white > CMYK
> gloss. In this case, for white print data and CMYK print data, select the checkbox for [Cut Con-
trols]>[Cutting Options]>[Return to Origin After Print], and then perform pullback printing.
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.
Unlike nested jobs, the layout of the print data cannot be changed for grouped jobs. Therefore, printing
such as that described under Reducing the Amount of Media Used(P. 70) is not possible, even if the print
data is grouped.
Procedure
1.
Configure settings individually for each job as necessary.
2.
Select multiple jobs from the job list.
The method for selecting multiple jobs depends on the OS.
Job groups are grouped in the order they are selected. To specify the printing order, select the jobs in the
printing order.
MEMO
3.
Perform one of the following operations to group the jobs.
• Right-click the selected multiple jobs, and then click [Compile Jobs]>[Multiple Job Grouping].
• With multiple jobs selected, click
 on the right side of the job tool area, and then click
[Multiple Job Grouping].
4.
Select and print the job group.
Processing by the RIP and printing are performed.

## Page 78

Adding a Name to a Queue Folder
Adding a name to a queue folder will make it easier to organize jobs. The queue folder name is retained
even after exiting VersaWorks. However when the registered machine is deleted, the queue folder names
are also deleted.
Some usage examples are listed below.
• Separating according to client (example: Queue A > Roland DG, Queue B > DGSHAPE)
• Separating according to used media name (example: Queue A > transparent media, Queue B > PVC
media)
• Separating according to work day (example: Queue A > 10/13, Queue B > 10/14)
Procedure
1.
Click
 for the queue folder to configure settings.
The [Queue Properties] appears.
2.
Enter the desired name in [Queue Name].
3.
Click [OK].
The [Queue Properties] window closes, and the entered name is displayed in Queue.
• The size of the queue folder tabs changes to match the width of Queue with the longest name.
• The number of Queue items that are displayed depends on the width of the window.

## Page 79

Saving and Reusing Printed Jobs
You can export the information and RIP processed data in the printing settings of a printed job and manage
it as a file in a separate folder.
The three types of files that can be saved are the following.
• [VW Job Files]
This file contains only the printing settings such as the job's source data (print data) information, the
media width, the number of copies to print, and the profile. The file takes up the least space.
When saving as a [VW Job Files], be sure to also save the source data. If the source data is not
present, it will not be possible to load the [VW Job Files].
• [VW Job + Source File]
This file contains the [VW Job Files], in which the printing setting information is saved, and the source
data. Saving this file makes it possible to print the same data with the same printing settings again.
If printing was performed from the design software, it is necessary to save in [VW Job + Source File]
format.
• [Native file]
This file contains RIP processed data. Although this file takes up the most space, it reduces the
processing performed by the computer and shortens the work time when printing the data again.
It is also possible to manage using a VersaWorks job list rather than managing as a file. The characteristics
of each are as shown below.
Item
Managing as a job list
Managing as a file
Management
Source data is managed in a separate folder, how-
ever the printing settings and other information are
managed using a job list.
The source data, printing settings, RIP data, and
other information are managed together in a fold-
er.
Reprinting
Reprinting is easy because there is a job that was
processed by the RIP.
Import into VersaWorks again in order to reprint.
New print data
Scroll the job list and access the new print data
that is added at the bottom.
Access to new print data is easy because the job
list can be made empty.
Saving a VW Job File
Saves only the printing settings information of the job (such as the job's source data information, the
media width, the number of copies to print, and the profile) as a file.
• When saving a [VW Job Files], be sure to also save the source data. If the source data is not present, it will not be
possible to load the [VW Job Files].
• If printing was performed from the design software, it is necessary to save in [VW Job + Source File] format.
P. 81 Saving a VW Job + Source Data File

Saving a VW Job + Source Data File

performed from the design software, deleting the job also deletes the source data at the same time. Because the
source data is required when reloading a VW job, save the job in the [VW Job + Source File] format so that you can
load the source data.

## Page 90

Reusing Job Settings
The print settings of a given job (job A) can be used with a different job (job B) that already exists in the
job list. In this way, the same print settings can be applied to different print data.
Most print settings can be reused, but there are limitations on the following items.
Items that cannot be used (Setting value returns to the default value.)
[Layout]
[Print Properties]
[Page Range]
[from]
[Exit]
[Orientation]*1
[Clip and Tile]
All items
[Step & Repeat]*2
All items
 
*1
 is set regardless of the job A and job B settings.
*2
This menu is set by right-clicking the job or by clicking
 on the left side of the job tool area.
 
Items where the job A settings are not applied and the job B settings remain (Setting value remains the
same as before reuse.)
[Layout]
[EPS]
[Margin]
[Color Replacement]
　
[Spot Colors] list
[Special Color Settings] list
[Variable Data]
All items
 
 
Items that take on the setting values of the leading page of job A
[Layout]
[Scaling]
[Scale]
[Position]
[X]
[Y]
 
 
Using Job Printing Settings with Other Jobs
Procedure
1.
From the job list, select the job (job A) whose print settings will be copied.
2.
Right-click the selected job, and then click [Copy/Paste Setting Value]>[Copy].
3.
From the job list, select the job (job B) in which to paste the setting values.
4.
Right-click the selected job, and then click [Copy/Paste Setting Value]>[Paste].
The job B settings are updated with the details of job A.
Reuse Settings

## Page 94

Adding Trim Marks or Margin Marks
Add trim marks, margin marks, or special cut marks around the image.
Type
Mark
Details
[Trim Marks]
Prints trim marks added at the four corners of the image as shown
in the diagram.
[Margin Marks]
Leaves margins around the image and prints the margin marks at
the four corners of the margin as shown in the diagram.
Set the horizontal margin with [Horizontal] (1) and the vertical
margin with [Vertical] (2) for the image.
[FOTOBA Cutline Marks]
When a Fotoba cutter is in use, adds specialized cut marks before
printing. Set the positions in which to print the marks and the spac-
ing between the image and the marks.
[KALAXY Cutline Marks]
When a Kala cutter is in use, adds specialized cut marks before
printing. There are two types of cut marks. To match the specifica-
tions of these marks, specify the horizontal line type and whether
to add a stop mark.
[KALA RoXY Cutline Marks]
[Neolt Cutline Marks]
When a Neolt cutter is in use, adds specialized cut marks before
printing.

Adding Job Properties
The model name and serial number of the printer that was used, as well as the job name, are printed
below the printed item. The following items can also be added. The printing size can be checked in the
layout area in the [Settings] window.
[File Name]
Print data file name
[Layout]
Number of copies and scale
[Quality Settings]
Current quality settings
[Color Settings]
Details of the [Color Settings]
[Printer Color Match]
Information of the color match between the reference color and the target color
[File Format]
Settings of the [Spot Color] and [Special Color] (only when the conversion is enabled)
[Device Controls]
Feed calibration (only when [Use Printer Settings] is not selected)
[Job Management]
Job handling after printing
[Clip and Tile]
Location of the tile (clipped location and tile size for a clipped image)
[Special Color]
Spot color plate generation or correction
[Date/Time]
Date and time that the job was processed
[Notes]
Text entered in the input field

## Page 99

Adding an Overlap Width for Tiled Printing
When dividing a large job for printing (tiled printing), add an overlap width to the divided part (tile).
The position for adding overlap (A) can be set individually from top, bottom, left, or right. Specify
whether to print the overlap line (B). If printing, specify the stroke width and color. Trim marks (C) are
added to the left and right of the overlap.
A
Overlap
• Specify the position for adding overlap in [Position].
• Specify the overlap width between 0.5 and 100 mm (0.02 and 3.93 in.) in
[Size] (D).
B
Overlap line
• Overlap is added when [Print Overlap Lines] is selected.
• Specify the overlap line width between 0.1 and 0.5 mm (3.94 and 19.68
mil) in [Thickness].
• Specify the overlap line color in [Color Settings].
C
Trim mark *1
Specify the trim mark length (E) between 0 and 10 mm (0 and 0.39 in.).
 
*1
Used when overlapping and applying media, then cutting along the trim mark to remove the overlapped portion.
 
This section explains how to configure the settings for jobs, not queue folders.

1.

• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
2.
Click
 [Clip and Tile].
3.
Follow Tiling an Image(P. 106) to divide the image into tiles.
4.
Specify the overlap position in [Position] under [Overlap].
• When applying from lower right
Printing with Added Marks and Information

## Page 100

Creates overlap widths at the top and left of tiles. Set this when applying the lower-right
(yellow) panel first, followed by the remaining three panels.
• When no application order is determined
Sets overlap on all four sides. Application can be started from any tile.
• When applying to two panels
Sets overlap from the bottom. Set this when overlap is required only vertically, not horizontal-
ly.
5.
Configure the advanced overlap settings.
• [Size]
Specifies the overlap width.
• [Print Overlap Lines]
Turn this on to print overlap lines.
• [Thickness]
When printing overlap lines, specify the overlap line width.
• [Color Settings]
When printing overlap lines, specify the overlap line color using the value of [CMYK] or [RGB].
6.
When printing trim marks, specify [Cut Line Length].
When trim marks are not necessary, specify 0 mm (0 in.).

## Page 102

Adding Color Bars
Print a color bar on the right side or left side, or color bars on both sides of the job to improve the
discharge stability. Ink is discharged from all nozzles during printing, preventing the nozzles from clogging.
• In the preview image, color bars are displayed with four colors (CMYK) regardless of the ink types of the printer. The
actual printed color bars will differ from those displayed in the preview.
• [Spot Color] is printed with horizontal alignment in an order such as CMYKWhGlPr.
• If the same color is used in two slots, such as with CMYKWhWhGlGl, the width of that color's color bar is doubled.
• Optimizer ink is printed under CMYK.
• Color bars are printed outside marks such as Fotoba marks.
MEMO
Procedure
1.
Perform the operation shown below according to what is being set.
• Job
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
The [Settings] window appears.
• Queue folder: Click
 for the queue folder.
The [Queue Properties] appears.
2.
Click
 [Mark].
3.
Set the following with [Color Bar].
• Color bar positions
Specify the positions with the [Left] and [Right] checkboxes.
• Color bar width
Use [Width] to specify the total width of each color bar.
• Spacing between color bar(s) and image
Use [Spacing] to specify the spacing between the color bar(s) and the image.
4.
Click [OK].
• Any changes made in [Settings] will be applied, and you will be returned to the main window.
• When changes are made in [Queue Properties]:
• When a message for saving the printing settings appears, click [OK].
• To apply the changes to a job already in the queue folder, right-click the job and click
[Copy/Paste Setting Value]>[Apply Current Queue Settings]. The changes are reflected in
the layout area of the main window.

.

## Page 105

Maintaining the Clipped Position
Print a clipped image while maintaining its original position. You can reprint only the necessary part of the
same print data.
As an example, this explains printing to multiple objects to be printed on using a flat table model.
1. Place the jig (A) on the flat table, and position eight objects to be printed on.
2. Create print data (B) for the jig size, and perform printing.
3. Because a problem (C) occurred during printing, position an object to be printed on only in that
position (D) and perform reprinting (E).
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.
Procedure
1.
Use one of the methods below to open the [Settings] window.
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
2.
Click
 [Clip and Tile].
3.
Select the [Clip Image] checkbox under [Clipping].
4.
Part for reprinting: Set the clip area (A).
Either drag the red points in the layout area or use [Top Left Position] and [Size] under [Clipping]
and set the clip area.
Specifying the Printing Area, Print Count, and Overprinting Count When Printing
104
 Printing Settings

## Page 106

5.
Select the [Maintain Clipped Position] checkbox.
You can print a clipped image maintained at its original position.
If this setting is not made, the clipped image will automatically be placed in the origin position.
IMPORTANT
[Maintain Clipped Position] cannot be set in the following situations:
• Tiling has been set.

Tiling an Image
When printing an image that will not fit on the width of the roll media, or when printing divided onto
multiple media (objects to be printed on) with a flat-bed printer, configure the tile position and size
settings.
Procedure
1.
Use one of the methods below to open the [Settings] window.
• Double-click the job to set.

 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].

The image in the layout area is zoomed in on.
3.
In [Tiling], select one of the tiling methods shown below.
• [Manual Tiling]
Sets the number of tiles and the tile size. This print data is tiled by a blue vertical line
according to the media size. Next adjust the position so that a face is not divided.
a. Align the pointer with the tiling line (blue line) in the layout area preview.
Align the pointer with the border line (red line) and drag to the inside to clip the image and change
the part that is printed.

## Page 108

If you want to further divide the image horizontally, align the pointer with the red line on
the right edge or left edge and drag the line.
c. To divide the image divided vertically also, align the pointer with the red line at bottom
edge or top edge of the preview and drag the line.
• [Tile Size]
Divides the job evenly at the specified size with the origin at the upper-left corner of the
media. The tiles on the right and bottom edges may be smaller than the specified size.
Select the tile size from [Size]. If you want to set a size other than the standard sizes, select
[CustomCUT] or [CustomROLL], and then enter the width and height.
• [Number of Tiles]
Divides the job into the number of tiles specified with [Horizontal] and [Vertical]. All tiles
have the same size.
Select the [Place alternately] check box to rotate the tiles in even-numbered columns 180
degrees. This will make the seam color difference less visible when the tiles are pasted
together.

Printing Specific Layers of the Print Data
For print data in PDF format with a layered structure (version 1.5 or later), each layer can be separately
set to be printed or not printed. When creating the print data with design software, create multiple layers
in advance. Doing so will allow setting of whether or not to print a white plate for each media color, and
separate the cutting lines by layer and set whether or not to perform cutting.
The following is an example showing print data with five layers. By combining layers, a variety of printing
is possible depending on the printer and ink type.

## Page 116

What Is Variable Printing?
Variable printing is printing which replaces a portion of the printing area such as characters and images,
for each printing. Applications include the printing of employee ID cards where the name, department,
and photograph differ; tickets with consecutive numbers, and postcard addressee information.
In order to perform variable printing, you will need the following files:
• Background data
• Data file for specifying the text or images to print
• Image files if you want to print images.
As an example, this section introduces how to create employee ID cards using a flatbed printer.
You can also use illustration software for Creating Templates with Illustration Software(P. 127), but this
requires tasks such as setting the layout and preparing barcodes in advance.
MEMO

## Page 133

Printing Multiple Jobs Layered on Top of Each Other
A layered job is one in which multiple jobs are layered on top of each other. With a layered job, multiple
jobs can be layered and printed with a single printing operation.
Two methods are available with layered jobs: "pull-back printing," in which the media is pulled back and
printed on, and "one pass multilayer printing," which prints on all layers together. One pass multilayer
printing may result in less printing misalignment than pullback printing.
However, one pass multilayer printing may not be possible depending on the model you are using or the
job.
1. Overlapping Multiple Jobs to Create a Single Job
Select multiple jobs from the job list and create a layered job.
Printing and cutting layered jobs
• The only cutting lines that are enabled are those on the top layer.
• When creating cutting lines in VersaWorks, add them to the top layer.
• When a layer job is generated, [Cut Controls]>[Operation Mode] is set to [Print Only].
MEMO
Procedure
1.
Select multiple jobs from the job list.
The method for selecting multiple jobs depends on the OS.
In a layered job, the layers are overlaid in the order they are selected. To specify the layer position, select
the jobs in order from the bottom layer.
MEMO
2.
A layered job can be generated by either of the following methods.
• Right-click and click [Compile Jobs]>[Generate Layered Job].
• Click
 on the right side of the job tool area, and then click [Generate Layered Job].
• When performing pullback printing, proceed to 2.  A: Performing Pullback Printing(P. 133).
• When performing one pass multilayer printing, proceed to 2.  B: Performing One Pass Multilayer
Printing(P. 134).
Printing Jobs Layered on Top of Each Other
132

## Page 135

2. B: Performing One Pass Multilayer Printing
With some models, one pass multilayer printing is possible for layered jobs with the following three-layer
configurations. Perform pullback printing for layered jobs with two-layer configurations or configurations
other than those listed below.
• [CMYK-> Wh -> CMYK]: When you want to, for example, print on transparent film and display the
printed document from both the printed surface and the opposite surface
• [Pr -> Wh -> CMYK]: When you want to, for example, improve the adhesion of ink to media that peels
off easily and express vibrant images using CMYK on a base of white ink
• [Pr -> CMYK -> Wh]: When you want to, for example, improve the adhesion of ink to media that peels
off easily, express vibrant images using CMYK on a base of white ink, and display the images from the
surface opposite the printed surface (the transparent surface)
• [Wh -> CMYK -> Gl]: When you want to, for example, print images on a base of white ink, eliminate
the effect of the media's color, and add textures and other expressions
You can print marks and job information on layered jobs when the [One Pass Multilayer Printing] checkbox is
selected.
• Marks are only added when the job on each layer has the same size, position, and orientation.
• Crop marks are added to the [Entire Area].
MEMO

 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
 [Settings].

The checkbox will be selected if the data can be used for one pass multilayer printing. [Current

the data cannot be used for one pass multilayer printing.

5.

You are returned to the main window.
6.

## Page 138

Printing on a Cylindrical Object
The rotary axis unit is used to print on a cylindrical object (media) while it is rotated.
If the media has a small diameter, reduce the number of nozzles (the feed length) to use. Measure the
diameter of the media in advance.
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.
Procedure
1.
Perform the operation shown below according to what is being set.
• Job
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
The [Settings] window appears.
• Queue folder: Click
 for the queue folder.
The [Queue Properties] appears.
2.
Click
 [Quality].
3.
Select [Rotary] for [Quality Settings]>[Media Name].
4.
Click
 [Printer Controls].
5.
Use [Rotary Controls] to set the number of nozzles to use in printing.
(1) Click [Automatic Calculation].
(2) Enter the [Media Diameter].
The value calculated from the media diameter is displayed next to [Nozzle Amount to Use in
Printing].
(3) Click [Apply].
(4) As necessary, adjust the number of nozzles to use.
Reducing the number of nozzles increases the printing quality but also increases the printing
time. Increasing the number of nozzles reduces the printing time but affects the printing
quality due to issues such as the generation of mist because the printed surface is far away.
Consider the printing quality and the printing time while making adjustments.
6.
Click [OK].
• Any changes made in [Settings] will be applied, and you will be returned to the main window.
• When changes are made in [Queue Properties]:
• When a message for saving the printing settings appears, click [OK].
• To apply the changes to a job already in the queue folder, right-click the job and click
[Copy/Paste Setting Value]>[Apply Current Queue Settings].
Printing Using the Rotary Axis Unit

7.
Perform printing.

Locations that are not printed on with structural ink will expand during heating in the oven after printing.
When creating the print data, set the locations that will be printed on with structural ink (locations that
will not expand).
Change [Quality]>[Structural Ink Settings] to [Spot Color] to switch the locations that will be printed on with structur-
al ink (locations that will not expand), as specified in the print data, to locations that will not be printed on with
structural ink (locations that will expand).
The output result will change greatly, so exercise caution with this setting.
MEMO
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.
Creating print data
The following methods are available for specifying structural ink in the print data.
• Roland DG Connect Designer
You can follow a procedure to create print data that specifies structural ink. You can also shorten the
process up to outputting with VersaWorks.
Connect Designer User's Manual
• Design software (Adobe Illustrator, CorelDRAW, etc.)
Use [RDG_STRUCTURAL] to specify structural ink for [Spot Color]. You can also use [Roland Structural
Texture System Library] for the structural ink library to create print data.
• [Spot Color]: Printing Using White Ink/Gloss Ink/Primer/Metallic Silver Ink/Structural Ink(P. 235)
• Library: Printing Using the Roland Color System Library(P. 227)
• JobAssistant
Add the [RDG_STRUCTURAL] spot color plate to the print data. You can duplicate and add existing
objects to spot color plates as well as convert these objects. You can also draw simple objects.
P. 244 Spot Color Plate Generation with [Job Assistant]
• Spot color plate generation function
Generate the [RDG_STRUCTURAL] spot color plate in the print data. The five patterns that you can
generate are [Print Area (Object-Based)], [Print Area (Color-Based)], [Outside Print Area (Object-
Based)], [Outside Print Area (Color-Based)], and [Entire Image].
P. 240 [Special Color Plate Generation] Function ([Settings])
Using existing print data
Replace the [Spot Color] specified in existing print data with [RDG_STRUCTURAL]. You can specify struc-
tural ink without correcting the print data.
P. 256 Assigning White/Metallic Silver/Gloss/Primer/Structural to a [Spot Color] in Existing Print Data
Printing Using Structural Ink

Printing with Smooth Gradations
To print smooth gradations, execute RIP and print in 16 bits. However, if the print data is in TIFF, JPEG,
or PNG format, it is not possible to execute RIP in 16 bits. In addition, 16-bit RIP processing requires more
time.
Procedure
1.
Perform the operation shown below according to what is being set.
• Job
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
The [Settings] screen appears.
• Queue folder: Click
 for the queue folder.
The [Queue Properties] appears.
2.
Click
 [Quality].
3.
Select [16-bit] for [Bit Depth] under [RIP Setting].
The default value is [8-bit].
4.
Click [OK].
• Any changes made in [Settings] will be applied, and you will be returned to the main window.
• When changes are made in [Queue Properties]:
• When a message for saving the printing settings appears, click [OK].
• To apply the changes to a job already in the queue folder, right-click the job and click
[Copy/Paste Setting Value]>[Apply Current Queue Settings]. The changes are reflected in
the layout area of the main window.

Media refers to general items to be printed on such as roll/sheet media to be printed on and thick objects
to be printed on with the flat-bed printer. In VersaWorks, media refers to items to which settings allowing
for printing on the above items have been added. Select from [Quality]>[Quality Settings]>[Media Name]
in [Queue Properties] or [Settings].
Media List
This is the list of [Media Name]. The displayed details vary depending on the model.
Category
Media name
Description
Solvent
[Generic Vinyl 1]
This is a profile for general PVC film.
[Generic Vinyl 2]
This is a profile for general PVC film.
If printing with [Generic Vinyl 1] results in noticeable smudges, use this
profile to print.
[Generic Banner 1]
This is a profile for general banner media.
[Generic Banner 2]
This is a profile for general banner media.
If printing with [Generic Banner 1] results in noticeable smudges, use
this profile to print.
[Generic Paper]
This is the profile for general paper media.
[Generic Backlit]
This profile is specialized for decorative lights.
Printing is performed with [Overprint] set to 2.
[Generic Heat Transfer Sheet]
This is the profile for general heat transfer sheets.
[Generic Clear PET Film]
This is the profile for general transparent PET film.
[Generic Clear Vinyl]
This is the profile for general transparent PVC film.
UV
[Generic / Generic Sign Production]
This is a profile used in basic printing.
[Generic II / Generic Sign Light]
This profile uses less ink than general profiles.
Reducing the amount of ink used results in a more even print surface.
[Generic Sign Quality]
This is a profile used in basic printing.
If [Generic / Generic Sign Production] results in noticeable bands on
the print surface or similar issues, use this profile to print.
[Generic Distance]
This is the profile to use when the print heads are separated from the
target by a long distance.
[Generic Label / Text Decoration]
This profile is specialized for applications such as adding names to labels
and text.
If you want to print lowercase characters or similar text with greater
precision, use this profile.
[Generic Backlit]
This profile is specialized for decorative lights.
Printing is performed with [Overprint] set to 2.
[Generic Decal]
This profile is used to print on decals.
[Primer]
This setting is used to print with primer.
[Special Effect]
This setting is used to print with gloss ink.
You can configure the setting for a gloss finish, matte finish, or some-
thing similar.
[Rotary]
This is the profile to use when printing using a rotary axis unit.
[** with Special Effects]
This setting is used to print simultaneously with color ink and gloss ink.
[** with Primer]
This setting is used to print simultaneously with color ink and primer.
DTF
[Generic Film Dark]
This is the profile to use when the media to transfer to is a dark color.
A heavy white layer is printed, allowing colors to stand out without
being affected by the base material.

Customizing Media
In VersaWorks, various media settings (print quality for each mode, total number of paths, dot types,
total ink amount, printer profiles, etc.) are registered for each printer. You can create custom media by
editing these settings after duplicating one of the existing types of media. You can edit new or existing
media without duplicating it if [Attribute] is set to [User Defined] in the [Media Explorer] window for the
media.
Procedure
1.
Select the printer to use, and then click
 (the hamburger menu).
2.
Click [Media Explorer].
3.
Select the media name to edit.
If [Attribute] is set to [Genuine], use the following procedure to duplicate the media.
(1) Select the media to duplicate.
(2) Click
.
The [Copy Media] window appears.
(3) Enter the desired media name, and then click [OK].
The media is duplicated. The changed media name is displayed for [Media Name].
4.
Click
 to expand the list, and then select the quality, resolution, and ink mode to use.
5.
Click
 or double-click the ink mode to edit.

Change the [Print Quality] or [Quality Settings] ([Calibration Settings], [Total Ink Limit], and [Profile
Settings]).
For modes with three layers for layered jobs, click [Fine Tune and Apply], [Set Ink Limit], or
[Select Profile] to enable selection of [Layer]. Select a layer, and then edit it. However, [Print
Quality] is a common setting for the three layers.
7.
Click [OK].
The window changes.
8.
In the [Media Explorer] window, click [OK] again.

Name].

## Page 174

Applying Crop Marks Individually to Multiple Images
You can apply crop marks [For Each Row] or [For Each Data Entry] within a group of images. Although this
increases the time required for output, it also increases the accuracy when performing printing and cutting
or cutting with crop marks. Configure this setting for print data that includes cutting lines.
Depending on the model you are using, this setting may be disabled.
Procedure
1.
Use one of the methods below to open the [Settings] for a job that includes cutting lines.
• Double-click a job that includes cutting lines.
• Right-click a job that includes cutting lines, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job that includes cutting lines selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area,
and then click
 [Settings].
2.
Click
 [Layout].
3.
Set [Copy] under [Print Properties].
Set the number of copies to output.
4.
Click
 [Mark].
5.
Select the [Crop Marks (Print&Cut Alignment)] checkbox, and then choose [For Each Row] or [For Each
Data Entry].
• [For Each Row]: Crop marks will be applied with the row of images treated as a single group.
• [For Each Data Entry]: Crop marks will be applied to each individual image.
The preview window can be used to check how crop marks are applied.
6.
Click [OK].
You are returned to the main window.
Crop marks can be applied to nested jobs in the same manner. However, all the jobs must be the same size.

## Page 177

This section explains how to adjust the overall coloring of an image by selecting and customizing color
settings, or by adjusting color saturation, contrast, brightness, tone curves, and other properties.
Color settings are preset combinations of input profiles and matching methods necessary for color man-
agement. You can control the overall color of the printed material by setting these input profiles and
matching methods. In VersaWorks, you can perform color management by selecting the color settings.
You can adjust the density of each ink and the overall contrast and brightness as desired with [Level
Adjustment]. In addition, adjustments to the tone curve enable more subtle adjustments.

## Page 179

Selecting the Color Setting from the List
Select the color setting from the following options.
[True Rich Color Bright Vivid]
It has a wide color gamut, and produces bright colors with high saturation.
[True Rich Color Bright Soft]
It has a wide color gamut, and produces bright colors. Compared with [True Rich
Color Bright Vivid], it produces a more relaxed impression.
[True Rich Color Vivid]
It has a wide color gamut, and produces colors with higher saturation than [True
Rich Color Standard].
[True Rich Color Standard]
It has a wide color gamut, while producing natural-looking colors.
[True Rich Color Soft]
It has a wide color gamut, and produces an impression that is more relaxed than
[True Rich Color Standard].
[True Rich Color Deep Vivid]
It has a wide color gamut, and produces darker colors with high saturation.
[True Rich Color Deep Soft]
It has a wide color gamut, and produces darker colors. Compared with [True Rich
Color Deep Vivid], it produces a more relaxed impression.
[True Rich Color] / [True Rich Color 2] /
[True Rich Color 3]*1
Despite its high-saturation output, it has a wide color gamut, more naturally pro-
ducing colors such as gray and peach.
[True Rich Color 2] is enabled only for CMYK images.
[True Rich Color 3] is enabled for both CMYK and RGB images.
[Prepress general]
Applies the standard settings used for printing in general printing environments.
[Prepress U.S.]
Applies the standard settings used for printing in general printing environments in
the United States.
[Prepress EURO]
Applies the standard settings used for printing in general printing environments in
Europe.
[Japan Color]
Applies the standard settings used for printing in general printing environments in
Japan.
[MAX Impact]
Produces vibrant images suitable for signs.
[Sign & Display]
Does not use the profile embedded in the image data (the profile specified by the
image data). You can obtain good results by selecting this setting when you want to
print dark colors over the entirety of signs.
[MAX Density U.S.]
Color conversion just for the illustration is disabled and output is performed as
defined in the data. You can obtain good results by selecting this setting when you
make the color density a priority.
[MAX Density Japan]
Color conversion just for the illustration is disabled and output is performed as
defined in the data. This setting is almost the same as [MAX Density U.S.], but
output is performed while preserving the primary colors.
[Density Control Only]
Color conversion is disabled and output is performed as defined in the data. The
amount of ink is limited, so the ink does not overflow.
[Custom]
You can select the input profile and the matching method (color conversion) set-
tings individually.
[Custom High-Performance]
You can select the input profile and the matching method (color conversion) set-
tings individually. This assumes high performance.
[Full Page Conversion Mode]
Applies the same matching method for both raster and vector objects.
 
*1
[True Rich Color 3] with the broader color gamut is recommended. Select the same color setting when you set [True Rich
Color] or [True Rich Color 2] before updating VersaWorks and want to output the same color.
 
Procedure
1.
Perform the operation shown below according to what is being set.
• Job
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].

## Page 182

Adjusting the Tone Curve
Use the tone curve to fine-tune the tone and color of the image. The tone curve consists of a horizontal
axis (input value, before adjustment) and a vertical axis (output value, after adjustment).
The horizontal axis indicates the original brightness of the image. The vertical axis expresses the bright-
ness after adjustment. The left edge of the horizontal axis indicates white (the brightest part). The right
edge of this axis indicates black (the darkest part). The top edge of the vertical axis indicates black (the
darkest part). The bottom edge of this axis indicates white (the brightest part).
Generally, you only need to make minor adjustments to the tone curve when adjusting the tone and colors
of an image. Note that adjustments to the image tone and color by way of the tone curve work in addition
to the adjustments made with [Level Adjustment] and enable more subtle adjustments.
Procedure
1.
Perform the operation shown below according to what is being set.
• Job
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
The [Settings] window appears.
• Queue folder: Click
 for the queue folder.
The [Queue Properties] appears.
2.
Click
 [Color Management].
3.
Click
 next to [Tone Curves] under [Level Adjustment].
The [Tone Curves] setting window appears.
4.
Under [Channel], select the color whose tone will be fine-tuned.
When [Apply to All Colors] is selected, all colors can be adjusted at the same time.
5.
Edit the tone curve.

(1) Click a certain point on the tone curve (the black line).
A black dot (a control point) appears at the point you clicked. You can add multiple control

(2) Use one of the following methods to adjust the shape of the tone curve by moving the control
point:
• Drag the control point:
Move the pointer to the control point, and then drag to move it.
• Click the control point, and then enter the value in [IN] or [OUT]:
The adjusted result is applied immediately to the layout area preview display.
• If you have already adjusted the levels with [Level Adjustment], selecting the [Show All Channels]
checkbox displays under [Tone Curves] the curves of the colors that have undergone [Level Adjustment].
• When there are multiple control points, control points other than the one being dragged are fixed in place.
This makes it possible to select and adjust just a specific tone area.
• To initialize the tone curve, click [Reset].
MEMO

Applying the Tone Curve Adjustment to Other Jobs

## Page 185

Customizing the Color Settings
VersaWorks offers options to configure input/output profiles and matching methods with [Color Settings]
and [Specify Printer Profile and Ignore Default] to print as accurate a reproduction of the colors in the
source data as possible.
• Input profiles: Also known as simulation profiles, these are ICC profiles that determine the colors
output by the printer.
• Output Profile: Also known as printer profiles, these are ICC profiles that can be switched for different
printers or media to enable more accurate color reproduction.
• Matching method: There are four methods for performing color conversions: [Perceptual], [Colorimet-
ric], [Saturation], and [Absolute]. These methods determine how to adjust the colors or the overall
color gamut when there are colors that cannot be reproduced with the color gamut of the printer.

od] can be changed. In this case, [Preset] changes to [Custom].
4.
Select [Simulation Target Profiles] or [Matching Method].

## Page 187
