Characteristics of Each Matching Method
The characteristics of each matching method are as follows.
• [Perceptual]
Converts colors while maintaining tonal balance. The color values are changed, but their values rela-
tive to each other remain the same. Use this option if you want to emphasize the color balance/natu-
ralness of a photographic image or something similar. This is a basic setting.
• [Colorimetric]
Converts colors with emphasis on brightness. A color outside of the printer's color gamut is converted
to a close color within the printer's color gamut. Use this option in situations where the color accuracy
is emphasized while consideration is given for the white of the paper. In the printing industries in
Europe and America, there are cases where this is the standard setting.
• [Saturation]
Converts colors with emphasis on color vibrancy. A color outside of the printer's color gamut is convert-
ed to a color with the same saturation within the printer's color gamut. Use this option in situations
where color vibrancy is required more than color accuracy, such as with charts and graphs.
• [Absolute]
This option is not commonly used. Use it in situations where color accuracy is required, such as with
PANTONE or DIC used with logos, corporate colors, etc.
• [Calibration and Ink Limit]
Color conversion is disabled and printing is performed as defined in the data. The total ink is limited
by the ink limit settings in the media. Depending on the item selected under [Color Settings], this may
not be displayed.
P. 178 Selecting the Color Setting from the List
• [Calibration]
Color conversion is disabled and printing is performed as defined in the data. There is no ink limit,
so the surface of the media may be oversaturated. Depending on the item selected under [Color
Settings], this may not be displayed.
P. 178 Selecting the Color Setting from the List
Adjusting the Output Colors

## Page 193

Obtaining a Stable Output Color
Adjust for variation in the output colors resulting from changes over time with the same model or from
individual differences with different machines of the same model.
The output colors will vary slightly even on the same printer if the data is output at different times.
In addition, even with the same settings on the same models, the output colors will vary slightly from
one printer to another. The causes of such issues are changes to printers over time; slight individual
differences in items such as the printers, the ink, and the media; and temperature or humidity changes in
the operating environment.
By determining the reference colors and matching the output colors to the reference colors, you can
obtain stable output colors by reducing color variations caused by factors such as changes over time and
individual differences.
However the following conditions must be met.
• The same measurement instrument is used at all times.
• The measurement instrument is calibrated appropriately.
• The print settings such as the media, resolution, printing quality, and color settings must be the same
for the printer that output the reference colors (hereinafter referred to as the "reference printer") and
the color match target printer (hereinafter referred to as the "target printer").
• The "reference printer" and the "target printer" are the same printer or are the same model of printer
and the ink types are the same.
Examples of utilizing this function
• When you want to unify the output colors from multiple printers in order to produce a large-volume order in a short
time
• When you want to unify the output color for different production times such as regular small-lot production, or once
yearly production
MEMO

## Page 201

Matching Different Printer Output Colors
It is possible to match the output colors of printers that are different models. It is also possible when the
model is the same but the ink type is different.
A: target printer (color matching source), B: printer to be reproduced (color matching destination)
To match the colors on different machines with the same model and same ink type, perform Obtaining a
Stable Output Color(P. 192).
The flow of this operation is as follows.
1. Creating a simulation profile
Create a texture and output it from the printer to use as the color match source. Then measure the
test chart and create the simulation profile.
2. Creating and installing the output ICC profile
a. Create the media at the printer registered in VersaWorks where color matching will be per-
formed. For details, refer to "Step 1: Create New Media" in Registering New Media(P. 146).
b. Create a texture and output it from the printer where color matching will be performed. Then
measure the test chart and create the output ICC profile.
c. Configure the settings for the output ICC profile that was created on the media which was
created in Step 1. For details, refer to "Step 4: Registering a Profile" in Registering New Media(P.
146).
3. Installing a simulation profile
Install the simulation profile that was created in Step 1 in VersaWorks. Refer to Adding Simulation
Target Profiles(P. 200) for details.
4. Profile application and printing
Using VersaWorks, configure the profile settings on the printer where color matching will be per-
formed, and perform printing. Refer to Selecting the Printer Profiles(P. 202) for details.
For details of the operation, contact your authorized dealer.
This operation is complex due to the large number of procedures and the multiple pieces of software that
are used. To simplify color matching between different printers, an add-on that completes all operations
within VersaWorks is available. Consider making use of the section Matching Output Colors Between Differ-
ent Printers (Add-on)(P. 207).
Adding Simulation Target Profiles

1.
From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Simulation Profile Management].

 under [RGB] or [CMYK] according to the profile being loaded.

## Page 204

Output color matching can be achieved with Matching Different Printer Output Colors(P. 200) or Obtaining
a Stable Output Color(P. 192). However, please consider introducing add-ons to reduce the number of
processes and the time and effort required for color matching.
The three main add-ons are listed below.
• Color matching for changes in printer output color (including color matching of the same ink type on a
different machine)
P. 204 Obtaining a Stable Output Color (Add-on)
• Complete color matching of different models in VersaWorks
P. 207 1.  Creating a Simulation Profile (Add-on)
• Printer profile creation on VersaWorks

## Page 208

Matching Output Colors Between Different Printers (Add-on)
With Matching Different Printer Output Colors(P. 200), creating simulation profiles and printer profiles
(also called media profiles or ICC profiles) requires profile software, while color measurement and profile
settings are performed in VersaWorks, meaning multiple software applications are used. The process is also
complicated due to the large number of steps required.
To simplify color matching between different printers, an add-on that completes all operations within
VersaWorks is available. To use the add-on, the following conditions must be met.
• A measurement instrument has been registered in VersaWorks and is available for use.
• [Printer Color Match (Extended)] has been purchased.
As an example, Printer A is the target printer (color matching source) and Printer B is the printer to be
reproduced (color matching destination), and both have been registered in VersaWorks.
A printer not registered in VersaWorks, such as a third-party printer, can also be used as the target printer (A).
IMPORTANT
The flow of this operation is as follows.
1.  Creating a Simulation Profile (Add-on)
2.  Printing with Simulation Profiles (Add-on)
If the color matching is not satisfactory, proceed to 3.
3.  Creating a Printer Profile (Add-on)
4.  Printing with Printer Profiles (Add-on)
However, if the media is predetermined and you want to improve color accuracy for PANTONE, DIC, etc., it
is more efficient to start from 3.  Creating a Printer Profile (Add-on).
1. Creating a Simulation Profile (Add-on)

1.
Prepare the measurement instrument and start the color matching menu.
(1) Set the measurement instrument.

(2) Click
 (hamburger menu) of the target printer in the main window, or
 to the right of the
target printer on the home screen.
(3) Click [Printer Color Match (Extended)].
(4) Select [Configure Color Match Settings with a Different Model], and then click [Next >].
The window changes to [Color Match Settings for a Different Model].
(5) Click [Add Color Match Setting for Different Models].

Spot Color
221

## Page 223

A [Spot Color] is a special color that is not a process color (CMYK). In this manual, "special mixture
colors such as DIC and PANTONE," "colors for which a library has been created in VersaWorks," "Roland DG
Corporation white ink, gloss ink, primer, metallic silver ink, and structural ink," and "areas specified for
variable printing" are called [Spot Color]. The main features are shown below.
• Special mixture colors such as DIC and PANTONE are used when specifying company image colors or
similar colors. It is also possible to select a [Spot Color] from the color samples and specify the color
that you want to express. VersaWorks converts the [Spot Color] explained above to process colors for
printing. However, because a [Spot Color] converted to process colors is affected by the ink, media,
and other factors, VersaWorks includes a function for adjusting the [Spot Color] output color.
• VersaWorks includes "spot color libraries" for CMYK inks. There is also a separate color library for
green, red, orange, and metallic silver inks. For gloss inks, texture and brio-coat libraries are also
separately prepared. It is also possible to create and use newly arranged spot color libraries.
• With a printer that includes white ink, gloss ink, primer, metallic silver ink, and structural ink, it is
possible to set these special inks as a [Spot Color] when printing. A set [Spot Color] is displayed as a
[Special Items] in the preview area.
• With variable printing which replaces some characters and images one at a time, the replacement
print area is specified using [Spot Color].
VersaWorks can only recognize a [Spot Color] in vector data. Colors used in raster data such as bitmap images cannot
be recognized.
MEMO
What Is [Spot Color]?

## Page 228

The Roland Color System Library is Roland DG's original spot color library. It consists of more than 1,000
[Spot Color].
When this library is used, you can easily create a color chart with your printer. In contrast to commercially
available color charts, this feature generates a color chart printed using "your printer" and "your media"
(slight color differences may occur depending on the conditions of the printer and media). By selecting
colors from this color chart and creating print data with these colors, you can accurately reproduce the
desired colors.
Step 1: Printing a Color Chart
Print a Roland Color System Library color chart.
The printer name, serial number, media type, ink type, and other information is printed in the color chart.
MEMO
Procedure
1.
Load into the printer the media on which the color chart will be printed.
2.
Click
 for the queue folder to be used.
The [Queue Properties] window appears.
3.
Click
[Quality], and then set [Quality Settings].
4.
Click
[Color Management], and then set [Color Settings].
When printing a color chart, the settings from the [Queue Properties] window are applied to the [Quality
Settings] and [Color Settings] setting items. You cannot change the settings using the [Settings] window after
the job has been added to the job list.
IMPORTANT
5.
Click [OK].
The [Queue Properties] window closes.
6.
Select the printer to use, and then click
 (the hamburger menu).
7.
Click the color chart that you want to print.
• [Color Chart Type-1]
The colors in the library are categorized by saturation and arranged to vary in hue in the
horizontal direction and in brightness in the vertical direction. To print this color chart in its
original size, you need A1-size or larger media.
Printing Using the Roland Color System Library
Spot Color
227

## Page 229

• [Color Chart Type-2]
The colors in the library are categorized by color tone and a color wheel is printed for each
tone. (Black colors are arranged linearly by brightness.) To print this color chart in its original
size, you need A1-size or larger media.
• [Color Selector]
Prints the ink combination chart. The chart shows the ink combination used for printing each
spot color. Although each page is printed in A4 size, you can cut the pieces along the trimming
marks and bind them in numeric order (outline numbers) to create a color sample book that
can be used to compare the colors.
• [Color Chips]
Prints 12 color chips per color for 5 colors in A4 size. The colors are categorized by color tone.
Click [Color Chips] and click the tone name to print the color chips for the selected tone.
[Color Chips] are printed with cutting lines so that the color chips can be trimmed and used individually.
If you print the color chips on media with a backing paper using a printer with a cutting function, you can
create color chip stickers by setting [Operation Mode] under [Cut Controls] to [Print & Cut].
MEMO
The following message appears: [The following job will be printed.Do you want to continue?]
Check whether the media loaded in the printer is large enough for the displayed page size.

## Page 231

Step 2: Registering Roland Color System Library Colors in Illustration Software
Load Roland Color System Library colors into the illustration software color palette. This is very useful, as
this allows you to easily specify the [Spot Color] from the color palette when creating print data.
Palette data for Adobe Illustrator and for CorelDRAW is available on the Roland DG Corporation website.
Register this data in the illustration software. Save these data files to the target computer (the computer
on which Adobe Illustrator or CorelDRAW is installed).
When Using Adobe Illustrator
This section explains how to register a color system library color in Adobe Illustrator.
With VersaWorks, in addition to the library registered here ("Roland Color System Library"), the following libraries and
[Spot Color] are prepared. Because it is possible to register for all off them with a single process, it is recommended
that you copy and paste all files.
• Roland Grapac Bri-o-coat Library
• Roland Green Color System Library
• Roland Orange Color System Library
• Roland Red Color System Library
• Roland Metallic Color System Library
• Roland Metallic Texture System Library
• Roland Texture System Library
• Roland Metallic Color
• Roland Structural Texture System Library
• Roland VersaWorks
MEMO
Procedure
1.
Access the following website.
https://downloadcenter.rolanddg.com/VersaWorks7#software
2.
Click [VersaWorks 7].
3.
Click [Illustrator].
Download Illustrator.zip according to the on-screen instructions.
4.
Open the [Swatch] or [Swatch Library] folder in the following folder on the target computer (the comput-
er on which Adobe Illustrator is installed).
• Windows: [C:\Program Files\Adobe\Adobe Illustrator 20**\Presets\(used language)]
• Mac: [Macintosh HD\Applications\Adobe Illustrator 20**\Presets\(used language)]

Extract the files from Illustrator.zip, and then save the file "Roland Color System Library.ai" in the folder

or] can be generated.*2

[Special Color Plate Generation] Function ([Settings])
Use the [Special Color Plate Generation] function to generate white, gloss, primer, metallic silver, or
structural spot color plates.
Step 1: Checking the Conditions That Permit Spot Color Plate Generation
First check the conditions that permit spot color plate generation. If a [Spot Color] is set on the print
data, that [Spot Color] cannot be generated by the [Special Color Plate Generation] function.
Procedure
1.
Select the job from the job list.
2.
Check [Special Items] from [Document Information] in the preview area.
When the following [Spot Color] is selected, that spot color cannot be generated.
•
: White plate
•
: Gloss plate
•
: Primer plate
•
: Metallic silver plate
•
: Structural plate
There are several other conditions under which spot color plates cannot be generated. The main conditions
are listed below.
• Vector data for which an arbitrary spot color name has been assigned to each white ink/metallic silver
ink/gloss ink/primer/structural ink

[RDG_WHITE] are used as [Spot Color].

## Page 250

Even when the size in the print data is the same, with actual printing contraction of the media, printer
tolerances, and other factors may result in misalignment of the spot color plates (white, primer, gloss,
etc.). Misalignment of white is particularly noticeable.
When spot color plate misalignment occurs, correct the misalignment by making fine adjustments to the
positions or by expanding/reducing the sizes. Print a test pattern in order to decide the correction values.
Depending on the printer, this function may not be displayed.
• Example 1
The white plate is displaced to the lower right. Use [Position Correction] to eliminate the misalign-
ment.
• Example 2
The white plate is printed too large. Use [Size Correction] to eliminate the misalignment.
Correcting the Position of Spot Color Plates
Correct the position of an image printed using spot color ink.
Depending on the printer, this function may not be displayed.
Procedure
1.
Open [Settings] for the job with the misaligned spot color plate.
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
2.
Click
 [Quality].
3.
Use [Mode] under [Quality Settings] to select the mode including the [Spot Color].
4.
Use [Special Color] under [Special Color Plate Correction] to select the [Spot Color] to correct.
5.
Click [Test Print] under [Position Correction].
6.
Select [Pattern Settings].
Adjusting the Position and Size of Spot Color
Plates
Spot Color
249

## Page 251

The test pattern has three stages in the plus direction and minus direction from 0 (no correction)
in both the scan direction and feed direction. The stages consist of simplified stages that are
coarse with units of 5, and detail stages that are fine with units of 1. The following test patterns
are expressed using a blue [Spot Color].
• [Simple (5)]
Select this when misalignment is large (when making rough adjustments to the position mis-
alignment).
• [Detailed (1)]
Select this when misalignment is small (when making detailed adjustments to the position
misalignment).
7.
Click [OK].
A test pattern is printed.
8.
Enter the [Horizontal] and [Vertical] correction values.
Select the value on the printed test pattern that makes the misalignment between the black line
and the [Spot Color] line the smallest. Enter the [Horizontal] and [Vertical] correction values
written in the test pattern.
The test pattern correction values differ for each job. Set the default values for each job.
IMPORTANT
9.
Click [OK].
You are returned to the main window.
When size correction is also necessary, next perform size correction.
Adjusting the Position and Size of Spot Color Plates

## Page 254

Replacing or Fine-tuning the Output Color of a [Spot Color]
The output color of [Spot Color] can be fine-tuned or replaced as necessary. The recommended methods
for adjusting a [Spot Color] to match the desired color are using [Nearest Color Finder] and finding the
desired color from a color chart output from the printer.
• Adjusting the Output Color of a [Spot Color]: [Nearest Color Finder](P. 260)
• Printing Using the Roland Color System Library(P. 227)
However, when replacing a [Spot Color] with a color from a different spot color library, or when making
fine adjustments to the color value of a process color, use these methods to change the spot color.
This is a setting that is configured for a specific job. To apply the setting to all subsequently added jobs,
configure the settings according to Assigning a Color from Another Spot Color Library to a Newly Created
Spot Color Library(P. 255).
Procedure
1.
Use one of the methods below to open the [Settings] window.
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
2.
Click
 [Color Replacement].
3.
Click [Details] under [Spot Colors].
The [Spot Color List] window appears.
You can check the following information with [Spot Color List].
[Spot Color Name]
Spot color name used in the job
[Library Name]
Name of the library that the [Spot Color] is registered in
[Library Output]
Color values defined in the library
If the spot color is one where the color type is defined in the Lab color space, a
caution mark (
) is displayed when this color cannot be reproduced (it is out of the
gamut) with the selected media and quality.
MEMO
[Delta E]
Color difference values from the reproducible range of colors*1
[Printer Output]
Process color values to which the spot color has been converted
If the [Spot Color] is one with a color type defined in the Lab color space, the values
converted with the method displayed in the [Matching Method] field on the right are
displayed.
MEMO
[Matching Method]
When setting multiple [Spot Color] at the same time, select with [Color Replacement]>[Match-
ing Method]. *2
 
Other Spot Color Functions

*1
Only when the library output color is out of the gamut
*2

4.
Use [Edit Output Color] to change the output color.
• [Use Spot Color Library]
Select the checkbox, then click [Use Color Library] and specify the color for replacement.
• Specifying the CMYK value directly
Change the CMYK value.
• Changing [Matching Method]
The matching method can be changed for a [Spot Color] that is defined in the Lab color space.
The CMYK values change depending on the selected method.
Make adjustments while checking the output result each time you change the color values.

## Page 256

Assigning a Color from Another Spot Color Library to a Newly Created Spot
Color Library
You can create [Spot Color] in newly created libraries and assign colors in existing spot color libraries.
The procedure for replacing the color of a [Spot Color] is the same as Replacing or Fine-tuning the Output
Color of a [Spot Color](P. 253), but if you configure the settings in this section, they will be applied to all
print data added in the future.
Procedure
1.
From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Spot Color Settings].
2.
Click
.
The [Create Spot Color Library] window appears.
3.
Enter the desired name in [Library Name].
4.
Select [Color Type].
The [Spot Color] that can be assigned are determined by the color space selected here.
• [Lab]
You can assign them to colors of libraries where the color type is Lab, such as DIC or PANTONE.
• [CMYK]
You can assign them to colors of libraries where the color type is CMYK, such as Roland Color
System Library.
• [Alias of Special Inks]
You can assign them to [RDG_WHITE], [RDG_Gloss], [RDG_PRIMER], [RDG_MetallicSilver], or
[RDG_STRUCTURAL].
5.
Click [OK].
A new spot color library is created.
6.
Select the newly created spot color library name and click
 on the right side of the window.
The [Spot Color] is added.
7.
Select the added [Spot Color], and then enter the [Spot Color Name].
8.
Select [Assign Another Spot Color].
9.
Click [Select].
The [Select Spot Color] window appears.
10.
Select [Spot Color Library].
11.
Select the spot color name to be assigned, and then click [OK].
The [Select Spot Color] window closes.
12.
Click [OK].
The [Spot Color Settings] window closes, and the created spot color library is saved.

Assigning White/Metallic Silver/Gloss/Primer/Structural to a [Spot Color] in Ex-
isting Print Data
Even when white ink, metallic silver ink, gloss ink, structural ink, or primer is specified with a spot color
name in existing print data, it cannot be recognized as a [Spot Color] if the spot color name is not the spot
color name designated by Roland DG Corporation. In this case, create a new spot color library and assign
the white ink, metallic silver ink, gloss ink, structural ink, or primer to these spot color names.
For example, even in the case below, [Spot Color] can be specified correctly even without making new
print data.
• The print data creator assigned original names.
• Print data created for use with RIP software other than VersaWorks is used.
Procedure
1.
Select the print data where the spot color is specified from the job list, and then use one of the methods
below to open the [Settings] window.
• Double-click the job to set.
• Right-click the job to set, and then click [Edit Design]>[Settings].
• With a job selected, click
 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
[Settings].
2.
Click
 [Color Replacement].
3.
Make a note of the spot color name that is displayed in [Color Name] under [Spot Colors].
Be sure to note the spot color name accurately.
4.
Click [OK].
5.
From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Spot Color Settings].
6.
Click
.
The [Create Spot Color Library] window appears.
7.
Enter the desired name in [Library Name].
8.
Select [Alias of Special Inks].
9.
Click [OK].
A new spot color library is created.
10.
Select the newly created spot color library name and click
 on the right side of the window.
The list is added to [Spot Color Name].
11.
Enter the spot color name that was noted in Step 3 into [Spot Color Name].
12.
Select the ink to apply from [Special Ink].
Select from [RDG_WHITE], [RDG_Gloss], [RDG_PRIMER], [RDG_MetallicSilver], and [RDG_STRUC-
TURAL].

Importing a [Spot Color] and Creating a Spot Color Library
Import a [Spot Color] from print data and create a spot color library. Output colors can be assigned to the
imported [Spot Color].
When output colors are assigned to [Spot Color] here, they will be applied to all subsequently added
pieces of print data. To apply only to that print data, configure the settings in Replacing or Fine-tuning the
Output Color of a [Spot Color](P. 253).

1.
From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Spot Color Settings].
2.
Click
.
The [Create Spot Color Library] window appears.
3.
Enter the desired name in [Library Name].
4.
Select [Color Type].
The [Spot Color] that can be assigned is determined by the color space selected here.
• [Lab]
You can assign them to colors of libraries where the color type is Lab, such as DIC or PANTONE.
• [CMYK]
You can assign them to colors of libraries where the color type is CMYK, such as Roland Color
System Library.
• [Alias of Special Inks]
You can assign them to [RDG_WHITE], [RDG_Gloss], [RDG_PRIMER], [RDG_MetallicSilver], or
[RDG_STRUCTURAL].

Click [OK].
A new spot color library is created.
6.
Select the newly created spot color library name and click
 on the right side of the window.

Select the print data that contains the [Spot Color] to import, then click [Open].

Select the [Spot Color] to import and click [OK].

spot color is added to the right side of the window.

• Select the added [Spot Color], and then enter the [Spot Color Name].

Click [OK].

If the ink, media, or printer has been changed, the output color of the [Spot Color] may not be as
intended.
Use [Nearest Color Finder] to adjust the output color of the [Spot Color]. The [Spot Color] to be adjusted
can also be specified according to measurement color, [Spot Color] in the spot color library, or ink ratio.
First, print the chart of the colors nearest to the [Spot Color] used in the job or to the specified [Spot
Color]. The chart can then be used to find the desired color and apply it to adjust the color to the
intended output. There are charts for visualization (left) and charts for measurement (right).
The applied color can be registered in [Nearest Color Finder Library] and reused.
Step 1: Printing the [Spot Color] Chart

1.
Select the job to set.

Use one of the methods below to open the [Nearest Color Finder] window.

 on the left side of the job tool area, and then click
 [Nearest Color Finder].
The [Spot Color] included in the job is displayed on the [Start Nearest Color Finder] window.
3.
Select the [Spot Color] to adjust.
Up to 10 colors can be selected.
Click [Change Print Quality] to change the [Quality] item of the job. After changing, click [OK] to return to
the previous window.
MEMO
4.
Click [Start].
The [Nearest Color Finder] window appears. When multiple [Spot Color] are selected, the nearest
color is set for each [Spot Color].

Select one of the following for the color being adjusted.

[Nearest Color Finder]

• Leave [Specify Color to Match] unchecked and proceed to Step 7 if you do not want to specify
a color to adjust.
• Proceed to the next operation if you want to specify a color to adjust.

b. Select the method for specifying the color.
• [Measure]: Specify using a measurement instrument or by inputting the Lab value.
• [Library]: Specify a [Spot Color] in a separate spot color library registered in Versa-
Works.

Specify the [Chart Format].

parison.
• [Align for Measurement]: Prints a chart that can be measured by a measurement instrument
for color charts.
7.
Specify the [Print Chart].
• If [Align for Visualization] was selected, select [Swatch Amount] from the following.
• [Few]: 3 swatches × 3 swatches × 3 pages = 27 swatches
• [Standard]: 5 swatches × 5 swatches × 5 pages = 125 swatches
• [Many]: 7 swatches × 7 swatches × 5 pages = 245 swatches
When [Align for Measurement] is selected, [Standard] will be selected.
MEMO
• Select [Swatch Interval] from the following.
• [Small]
• [Standard]
• [Large]
[Swatch Interval] is the color difference from the neighboring patch. Selecting [Small] makes the color
difference smaller. Selecting [Large] makes the color difference larger.

8.
Under [Print Chart], click [Print].

on the [Nearest Color Finder] window.

## Page 263

Step 2: Selecting and Applying the Desired Color
When Printing [Align for Visualization] Charts
Procedure
1.
View the nearest colors chart on each tab to check whether it contains the desired color.

click [Print Chart Again] to print the chart centered on that swatch.
2.
In the [Nearest Color Finder] window, select the desired color.
To do so, select the number on the chart with [Swatch Number] or select the swatch with the
corresponding number on the chart window.

Click [Save to Library].
The selected color is applied to the selected spot color on the tab in the [Start Nearest Color
Finder] window. However, it will not be applied to jobs with a different [Quality] setting.
4.
Specify the color on all tabs.

Click [Exit].

as intended.

ences]>[Measurement Instrument] on the menu bar.

Mac: [Preferences] is displayed as [Preferences].

Click [Select with Measurement Instrument] and measure the chart.

Click [Save to Library].

Color Finder] window. However, it will not be applied to jobs with a different [Quality] setting.

Specify the color on all tabs.

Click [Exit].

## Page 269

Create and use an original spot color library in a [Spot Color] library.
Example of use
1. Assign a spot color name to a color that has not been decided at the time of print data creation and
create the data.
2. Create a spot color library and define the spot color name that was created in 1.
3. Add the print data to the job list.
4. Perform printing.
Also, there is no need to recreate the print data when changing the specified [Spot Color]. To print again
with a different color, use [Spot Color Settings] to define one [Spot Color] in multiple spot color libraries
in advance, and then switch the spot color library you are using.
There are three types of [Color Type] for created spot color libraries.
• [Lab]
Specify a [Spot Color] using a Lab value. Specify by entering the value directly or by specifying the
value measured by the measurement instrument. Select [Assign Another Spot Color] to specify any
color in all spot color libraries, such as DIC and PANTONE.
• [CMYK]
Specify a [Spot Color] using a CMYK value. Select [Assign Another Spot Color] to specify any color in
the Roland Color System Library.
• [Alias of Special Inks]
Converts a [Spot Color] to a white, gloss, primer, metallic silver, or structural spot color ink.

From the menu bar, click [Edit]>[Spot Color Settings].

Click
.

Enter the desired name in [Library Name].
The set library name cannot afterwards be changed.

Select [Color Type].
• [Lab]
• [CMYK]

• When the CMYK value is decided, select [Define Output Values] and enter the CMYK value.
• When specifying a [Spot Color] that is defined by the CMYK value, such as Roland Color
System Library, select [Assign Another Spot Color] and click [Select] to specify the [Spot
Color].
• [Alias of Special Inks]
When converting to a white, gloss, primer, metallic silver, or structural [Spot Color], specify
the [Spot Color] from [Special Ink].
• White: [RDG_WHITE]
• Gloss: [RDG_Gloss]
• Primer: [RDG_PRIMER]
• Metallic silver: [RDG_MetallicSilver]
• Structural: [RDG_STRUCTURAL]
9.
Click [OK].
The settings are saved, and you are returned to the main window.
Be sure to click [OK]. If you click [Cancel], a confirmation window appears to confirm that you want to discard
the changes. If you click [Yes] in this confirmation window, the window is closed and no changes made to the
settings or newly registered [Spot Color] are saved.

## Page 275

Switching the Spot Color Library to Use
By switching the spot color library to use, you can replace the [Spot Color] without correcting the print
data.
Procedure
1.
Create multiple spot color libraries.
In each spot color library, define a [Spot Color] used with the print data. Creating a Spot Color
Library(P. 268)
2.
Use one of the methods below to set the spot color library to use.
• Move the spot color library to the top of the list.
Select the spot color library to use, and then click [Move Up]. If you move it to the top-most
position of the libraries that define the same [Spot Color], the [Spot Color] of this top-most
library will be specified.
• Clear the checkboxes of the spot color libraries that will not be used.
Clear the checkboxes in the [Select] field for the spot color libraries that you do not want to
use.
3.
Add the print data to the job list.
In the job layout area, you can check the job whose [Spot Color] has been replaced.
Using a Spot Color Library
274
 Spot Color

## Page 276

Even if the color to be printed has not yet been decided, first create the print data with the spot color
names.
Using print data with the spot color names makes it possible to specify different colors for the spot colors
in VersaWorks without modifying the print data.
By duplicating a job in the VersaWorks job list, [Spot Color] values can be set with [Settings], or the [Spot
Color] values can be set without duplicating the job by creating a spot color library and following the
instructions under Switching the Spot Color Library to Use(P. 274).
Points to note regarding spot color names
• The spot color name "S" will be replaced by [RDG_STRUCTURAL].
• To stop replacement, click [Edit]>[Spot Color Settings] on the menu bar, and then turn off [VersaWorks Default
Alias] in the [Library List].
• When replacement is stopped, printing will be performed using the color value specified in the design software.
• When using Dimensor S print data in VersaWorks, make sure [VersaWorks Default Alias] is turned on.
Using Dimensor S Print Data in VersaWorks
• On models with Or, Gr, and Re inks, spot color names such as "Orange," "Green," and "Red" (with the first letter
capitalized) will not be recognized as spot colors.
They will not be displayed in the [Color Name] field under [Spot Colors] in [Color Replacement]. Colors with names
such as "orange" or "GREEN" will be recognized.
• Use single-byte alphanumeric characters for spot color names. Print data with spot color names using two-byte
characters or some symbols may not be displayed correctly in the [Color Name] field under [Spot Colors] in [Color
Replacement].
IMPORTANT

277

## Page 279

[Special Color] are special colors that are used in VersaWorks and are different from the [Spot Color] that
are widely used in the printing industry. While [Spot Color] are recognized based on the color name (such
as RVW-BK01A or PANTONE Yellow 012C), [Special Color] are recognized based on the color value (such as
C: 10, M: 20, Y: 30, K: 0, L: 50, a: 60, and b: 70). VersaWorks detects the specific colors in a job by color
value (RGB or CMYK) and replaces them with color values defined in VersaWorks (CMYK or Lab).
VersaWorks can only recognize a [Special Color] in vector data. Colors used in raster data such as bitmap images cannot
be recognized.
MEMO
What Is [Special Color]?
278
 Special Color

## Page 280

To use a [Special Color] in VersaWorks, first you must register a color (input color: RGB or CMYK) that you
want to use as a [Special Color] and the color (output color: CMYK or Lab) after replacement in [Special
Color Settings]. Then check the [Special Color] from [Color Replacement]>[Special Color Settings] and
perform printing.
C:0 M:100 Y:0 K:22
C:0 M:100 Y:0 K:22
C:100 M:40 Y:0 K:0
When the [Special Color] function is used, it is possible to convert the input color to a CMYK value or Lab value,
however when the output color after replacement is CMYK, it will not be the target for color matching.
P. 186 Characteristics of Each Matching Method
When printing a color (C: 0, M: 100, Y: 100, K: 0) through the regular workflow, color matching is performed and the
ink used may not be exactly (C: 0, M: 100, Y: 100, K: 0). However, when replacement with [Special Color] is performed,
the output colors are not targeted for color matching and printing will be performed using the specified CMYK values.
MEMO
Step 1: Set the [Special Color].
Make a note of the colors (RGB or CMYK) in the print data in advance. Then register the colors (CMYK or
Lab) after replacement.

1.

2.
Click
.
The [Create New Special Color Library] window appears.

Enter the desired name in [Special Color Library Name].
The set library name cannot afterwards be changed.
MEMO
4.
Click [OK].
A new special color library is created.
5.
Select the newly created special color library name and click
 on the right side of the window.
The [Input Value] and [Output Value] appear.
6.
Click
 under [Input Value] to select CMYK as the color space.

• Be sure to click [OK]. If you click [Cancel], a confirmation window appears to confirm that you want to
discard the changes. If you click [Yes] in this confirmation window, the window is closed and no changes
made to the settings are saved.
• As in this example, if you configure the [Output Value] as CMYK values, the color will be printed according
to the density curve configured in the [Media Explorer] window (and not with the [Matching Method]
setting). This method is useful in situations such as when you want to print a specific color without any
impurities. But in this case, the ink limit does not apply. Be cautious of excessive ink usage when printing.

Color

isting Media

Prints the color system library. Color samples of other

Color System Li-
brary
[Color Chart Type-1]
Prints the Color Chart. The queue folder where this job
is saved can be specified in the location where the job
is added.
The Color Chart is a chart that categorizes the colors
in the library by saturation, and arranges them to vary
in hue in the horizontal direction and in brightness in
the vertical direction. To print this color chart in its
original size, you need A1-size or larger media. Select
Type-1 or Type-2.
[Color Chart Type-2]
[Color Selector]
Prints [Color Selector]. The queue folder where this
job is saved can be specified in the location where the
job is added.
The [Color Selector] is the ink combination chart. The
chart shows the ink combination used for printing each
spot color. Although each page is printed in A4 size,
you can cut the pieces along the trimming marks and
bind them in numeric order (outline numbers) to create
a color sample book that can be used to compare the
colors.
[Color Chips]
Prints 12 color chips per color for 5 colors in A4 size.
The colors are categorized by color tone. Click [Color
Chips] and click the tone name to print the color chips
for the selected tone. With printers that are equipped
with a cutting function, cutting is performed for each
color to create a seal color sample book.
[Pure Color 1]
Prints [Pure Color 1]. The queue folder where this job
is saved can be specified in the location where the job
is added.

is saved can be specified in the location where the job
is added.

saved can be specified in the location where the job
is added.

can be specified in the location where the job is add-
ed.
[Dark]

can be specified in the location where the job is add-
ed.

## Page 302

[Quality]
Configures print quality and spot color plate settings for a job.
Item
Description
Refer to
[Quality Settings]
Sets the printing quality in detail.
[Media Type]
Sets the media type.
[Print Quality]
Select the printing quality. This determines the resolu-
tion.
The resolution is displayed according to the model.
Default value: [Standard] or [Production]
[Artistic]
[High Quality]
[Standard] or [Production]
[High Speed]
[Draft]
[Resolution]
This is decided according to the selected [Media Type]
and [Print Quality].
[Mode]
The selectable items vary depending on the machine ink
type and the selected [Media Type] and [Print Quality].
This lists the typical items for CMYK inks and spot color
inks.
The "p" in items such as "6p" and "8p" means "passes."
When the number of passes is higher, the print head
performs a larger number of passes for the same image.
This results in finer drawing and improves image quality,
however it increases the printing time.
(v) prints in variable dot modes (modes that use dots of
varying sizes).
HD is a mode that has a higher Wh ink density than
White(v).
CMYK(v)
This prints using CMYK inks only.
White -> CMYK(v)
This prints first with Wh ink followed by CMYK inks. This
allows printing that is not affected by the media color,
such as with transparent media.
CMYK -> White(v)
This prints first with CMYK inks followed by Wh ink. This
is used in cases such as printing on transparent media
when the image will be viewed from the opposite side
of the printed surface.
White(v)
This prints using Wh ink only.
White(v) HD
This prints with higher density Wh ink compared to
White.
CMYK -> MatteVarnish(v)
Printing with CMYK inks and gloss ink is performed in a
single pass.
The CMYK color inks are overprinted by the gloss ink.
The gloss ink has a matte finish.
GlossVarnish(v)
This performs gloss-finish printing with gloss ink.
Overprinting with gloss ink is performed after printing
with Wh ink and CMYK inks is completed.
Printing using only gloss ink is also possible.
MatteVarnish(v)
This performs matte-finish printing with gloss ink.
Overprinting with gloss ink is performed after printing
with white ink and CMYK inks is completed.
Printing using only gloss ink is also possible.
Embossing(v)
This performs embossed-finish printing with gloss ink.
Embossing with gloss ink is performed after printing
with white ink and CMYK inks is completed. The ink
thickness is determined by the number in [Quality Set-
tings]>[Details]>[Overprint].
Embossed printing using only gloss ink is also possible.

## Page 303

Item
Description
Refer to
Primer1(v)
This prints only using the primer.
The texture of the finish and ink adhesion are different
between Primer 1 and Primer 2. You can also perform
printing using Primer 1 and Primer 2 in turns.
Overlaying Primer 1 and Primer 2 may result in different
effects in the texture or adhesion from the case where
they are used individually for printing. The texture and
adhesion may vary depending on the media to be used.
Select an appropriate type according to their proper-
ties.
Primer2(v)
Primer -> CMYK(v)
Printing with primer ink and CMYK inks is performed in a
single pass.
The primer ink is overprinted by the CMYK color inks.
Primer -> White(v)
Printing with primer ink and Wh ink is performed in a
single pass.
The primer ink is overprinted by the white ink.
CMYK -> Wh -> CMYK
Use this item when you want to, for example, print on
transparent film and display the printed document from
both the printed surface and the opposite surface.
P. 132 Printing
Multiple Jobs
Layered on Top
of Each Other
Pr -> Wh -> CMYK
Use this item when you want to, for example, improve
the adhesion of ink to media that peels off easily and
print vibrant images on a base of white ink.
Pr -> CMYK -> Wh
Use this item when you want to, for example, improve
the adhesion of ink to media that peels off easily, print
vibrant images on a base of white ink, and display the
images from the surface opposite the printed surface
(the transparent surface).
Wh -> CMYK -> Gl
Use this item when you want to, for example, print im-
ages on a base of white ink, eliminate the effect of the
media's color, and add textures and other expressions.

primer, metallic silver, and structural) in VersaWorks.
You cannot generate spot color plates with print da-
ta where all of [RDG_WHITE], [RDG_GLOSS], [RDG_PRI-
MER], [RDG_MetallicSilver], and [RDG_STRUCTURAL] are
used in the illustration software. When [RDG_WHITE],

generated only for the unused [Spot Color].

[True Rich Color *]
Different colors are expressed by [Brightness] and

[True Rich Color 3]
Despite its high-saturation output, it has a wide color

and peach.
[True Rich Color 2] is enabled only for CMYK images.

[True Rich Color]
[Prepress general]
Applies the standard settings used for printing in gen-
eral printing environments.
[Prepress U.S.]
Applies the standard settings used for printing in gen-
eral printing environments in the United States.
[Prepress EURO]
Applies the standard settings used for printing in gen-
eral printing environments in Europe.
[Japan Color]
Applies the standard settings used for printing in gen-
eral printing environments in Japan.
[MAX Impact]
Produces vibrant images suitable for signs.
[Sign & Display]
Does not use the profile embedded in the image data
(the profile specified by the image data). You can
obtain good results by selecting this setting when you
want to print dark colors over the entirety of signs.
[MAX Density U.S.]
Color conversion just for the illustration is disabled
and output is performed as defined in the data. You
can obtain good results by selecting this setting when
you make the color density a priority.
[MAX Density Japan]
Color conversion just for the illustration is disabled
and output is performed as defined in the data. This
setting is almost the same as [MAX Density U.S.],
but output is performed while preserving the primary
colors.
[Density Control Only]
Color conversion is disabled and output is performed
as defined in the data. The amount of ink is limited,
so the ink does not overflow.
[Custom]
You can select the input profile and the matching
method (color conversion) settings individually.
[Custom High-Performance]
You can select the input profile and the matching
method (color conversion) settings individually. This
assumes high performance.

Visually select the color setting from seven color set-

Selecting the
Color Settings:
[Quick Setup]

Loads a saved Roland Color Simulation file (.rcs).
[Save...]
Saves the set [Color Settings] and [Simulation Target
Profiles] (Roland Color Simulation file (.rcs)).
[Advanced Settings]
[Use Embedded ICC Profile]
Select this checkbox to use the profile embedded in
the image data for color conversions (if such a profile

Profiles] for the image.
P. 187 Specify-
ing Color Set-
tings for Each
Object
[Matching Method]
Sets the color matching method.
Default value: [Colorimetric]
[Perceptual]
Converts colors while maintaining tonal balance. The
color values are changed, but their values relative to
each other remain the same. This setting is suitable
for photographic images.
[Colorimetric]
Converts colors with emphasis on brightness. A color
outside of the printer's color gamut is converted to
a color with the same brightness within the printer's
color gamut.
[Saturation]
Converts colors with emphasis on color vibrancy. A
color outside of the printer's color gamut is converted
to a color with the same saturation within the print-
er's color gamut. This setting is suitable for printing
charts and graphs.
[Absolute]
This option is not commonly used. Select this setting
for special uses such as printing logo colors.
[Calibration and Ink Limit]
Color conversion is disabled and printing is performed
as defined in the data. The total ink is limited by
the ink limit settings in the media. Depending on the
item selected under [Color Settings], this may not be
displayed.
[Calibration]
Color conversion is disabled and printing is performed
as defined in the data. There is no ink limit, so the
surface of the media may be oversaturated. Depend-
ing on the item selected under [Color Settings], this
may not be displayed.
[Simulation Target Profiles]
Selects the profile to use as the base for the color
you want to output.
[RGB]
Sets the RGB profile for data created with RGB and
ordinary photo data.
Default value: Roland_DG_WideGamut_RGB.icc
AdobeRGB1998.icc
Produces most of the RGB color gamut (range). This
setting is suitable for printing images with a wide
range of colors.
Ro-
land_DG_SignRGB_v2.icc
Produces vibrant images suitable for signs.

[Cut Controls]

Item
Description
Refer to
[Operation Mode]
Selects the printing and cutting operation mode.
VW7 First Step Guide
[Operation Mode]
[Print & Cut]
Select to perform printing and cutting continuously.
[Cut & Print]
This method uses print data including cutting lines to
perform cutting first followed by printing. Performing
cutting first reduces the drying time compared to cut-
ting after printing.
However depending on the conditions of the media after
cutting, there may be an effect on printing.
[Print Only]
Select to perform printing only.
[Cut Only]
Select to perform cutting only.
[Cutting Options]
Configures the cutting operation mode and detailed set-
tings.
Detailed settings default values: All cleared
[Cut All Paths]
All paths will be recognized as cutting lines and will be
cut regardless of whether they are drawn with the [Spot
Color] (CutContour).
This can be selected from [Queue Properties].
[Cut Image Boundaries]
Cuts the border of the job regardless of whether a cut-
ting line is present.
[None]
The border is not cut.
[Normal Cut]
The border is cut.
[Perforated Cut]
Perforated cutting is performed for the border.
[Return to Origin After Print]
Pulls back the media after printing.
[Cut Passes]
Specifies the [Cut Passes].
[Execute PREFEED Operation]
Feeds out media according to the size of the data be-
fore starting output, thereby preventing the media from
being pulled out with excessive force. The media is then
pulled back to the output-start location.
[Cutting Conditions Settings]
Configures printer cutting conditions such as the blade
force.

ing the Cutting
Conditions

Lines]
Configures the perforation and half-cut settings for per-
forated cutting.

[Perforation Length]
Sets the length of the part that will be cut through to

cally Creating a

[By Time]
When the specified time is reached, a nested job is cre-
ated. Processing by the RIP and printing are not started
until the nested job is created. Furthermore, if you set
[Set Time Interval], nested jobs are created according
to the set interval of time.
Default value: Cleared
[Time]
[Set Time Interval]
[By Paper Length]
Jobs are nested until the specified media length is
reached. A nested job is created when the specified
media length is reached. Processing by the RIP and
printing are not started until the nested job is created.
You also will not be able to check the state of nesting in
the layout window.
Default value: Cleared

tered in the queue folder reaches the specified value.

Processing after

pleted
[Action]
This section explains how to process a job after printing
is completed.
[Save Job]
Jobs will be saved after printing finishes. The data proc-
essed by the RIP is kept, so you can print the job again
without having to process it by the RIP.
[Delete RIP Data]
All intermediate data such as the data processed by the
RIP will be deleted, but the job will remain in the job
list. To print the job again, you will have to process it
by the RIP again.
[Delete Job]
Jobs will be deleted completely and will be deleted
from the job list.

with [Horizontal] and [Vertical]. All tiles have the
same size.
[Horizontal]
[Vertical]
[Place alternately]
The tiles in even-numbered columns will be rotated
180 degrees. This will make the seam color differ-
ence less visible when the tiles are pasted together.
[Overlap]
Sets the overlap width for divided images (tiles)
when dividing a large job and printing the divided
parts (tile printing).
P. 98 Adding an
Overlap Width
for Tiled Print-
ing
[Position]
Specifies the overlap position using the Up, Down,
Left, and Right icons.
Default value: No
[Size]
Specifies the overlap width.

## Page 324

[Variable Data]
Configures settings for replacing portions of the image to create output with unique images or text per
copy.
Item
Description
Refer to
[Variable Data Settings]
Loads a variable print data file and configures various
settings.
P. 115 What
Is Variable
Printing?
[Enable Variable Data Printing]
This checkbox can be selected when a file for variable
printing has been added to the job.
•
: Specifies and loads a data file.
•
: Saves the data file that was edited with the
variable data settings.
•
: Adds a line to the data file.
•
: Deletes a line from the data file.
Settings such as the text font and image alignment can
be configured when the [Enable Variable Data Printing]
checkbox is selected and a data file (file created with
csv or other means containing the path information of
the image file that you want to insert) is loaded.
[Type]
The item to edit varies depending on the target.
• [Image]
• [Text]
• [Barcode]
[Text]
When the data file is loaded, data that starts with
strings that denote paths (such as "C:\") is automatically
recognized as image data, otherwise it is automatically
recognized as text data.
[Font]
Selects the font type.
[FontSize]
Sets the font size.
[Tracking]
Sets the spacing between two characters. Set this to
100 to make the spacing one-character wide.
[Auto Sizing]
Select this checkbox to automatically adjust the font
size to fit the text in the variable field when the text is
larger than the variable field.
[Alignment]
Sets the alignment of the text in the variable field.
[Rotate]
Rotates the text in the variable field in units of 90 de-
grees.
[Mirror]
Horizontally mirrors the text in the variable field.
[Opacity]
Sets the opacity of the entire variable field. Set this to
0% to make the target transparent.
[Draw on White Back-
ground]
Select this checkbox to print the background object on
top when the background object and the variable field
overlap.
[Effect]
Click the following icons to set the text effect:
[Offset]
Sets the offset widths of shadow characters as percen-
tages.
[Stroke]
Sets the stroke widths of outlined characters in points.
[Color]
Sets the color of the text and the background color
inside the variable field.

[Color Settings]

[Barcode]

[Rotate]
Rotates the barcode or QR code in the variable field in
units of 90 degrees.
[Mirror]
Horizontally mirrors the barcode or QR code in the vari-
able field.
[Error Correction Level]
Select the correction level to ensure the QR code can
be read correctly even if part of the code is dirty or
missing. The percentage of missing data that can be
recovered increases in the order of L, M, Q, and H, but
the data size also increases.
[Field Special Color Plate Gen-
eration]
Generates spot color plates for the area.
[Generated Pattern]
Select the area for generating the spot color plate from
[Print Area (Color-Based)] or [Entire Field].
[Density]
Sets the spot color plate density.
